108. **Drawing (21.5, R3):** `drawRope` draws the 47 segments with `line()`, whose dotting now runs on across calls (`lineN`), so a polyline dots evenly: solid in the air, dotted and at 75% alpha under the surface. The ends are drawn at the drawn rod tip and lure. A taut pull adds a standing wave of (0.4 + 0.9 T) px; the heavy fish's hum shifts the underwater segments. Segments turned toward the moon or the dusk glow get a `bone` pixel at their middle. The fight keeps the tension tint (7.4). `drawPassiveLine` and `drawFightLine` are gone: the passive float and the fight use the same rope.
109. **Debug (21):** `dbg.rope()` returns `{mode, n, onSurface, len, span, taut, sagMax, ms}`; the overlay line is `rope pinned  len  taut  sag  ms`. `sailing.lureAt()` gives where the far end is held.
110. **Allocation (21.1):** the heap sampling profiler (CDP, 512-byte interval) attributes allocations to the rope code only while V8 warms up (about 15 KB in the first 30 s, number boxing before optimisation). After a minute's warm-up, 10 minutes of casting (95 casts: cast, sink, wait, reel in) sampled 0 bytes in `rope.js` and in `ropeStep`, `drawRope`, `drawCast`, `lurePos`, `floatPos` and `floatY`; the whole game sampled 494 KB in that time, elsewhere (mostly `sailing.draw` and `water.draw`, as before).
111. **The current (22.1, R4):** every water has a pure `drift(seed, t, depth, onBottom, wind)` in its data. The creek's is unchanged (11.1). The lakes share `G.data.lakeDrift` (defined in `loc_fog.js`, the first data file loaded) with `current: {lake: true, speed, wind, ramp, period, slack, bottom}`: Fogbound 0.4 + 0.35 × wind px/s, the Pines 0.45 + 0.3 × wind, slowing 75% toward the bottom; the direction is `tanh(3 cos(phase + 2π t / period))` (it turns once in 240 s on Fogbound, 200 s in the Pines, from a phase set by the cast's seed). The Red Room's is 0.12 px/s and turns round every 23 s. `wind` is the grey hours' share (`sailing.light(clock).grey`), so the path is still fixed by the seed, the clock and the fixed dt. The plan's `flow(...)` returning x and y became this horizontal drift; the vertical part (a lure lifted off the bottom) is the line's, in `lureStep`. Over 200 seeds a lake lure at 6 m drifts 3–10 px in 30 s for 89% (Fogbound) and 91% (the Pines); the rest drift less, cast while the wind is turning. Faster settings (0.48 and 0.5) put most casts over 10 px, so they were not kept.
112. **The line in the current (22.2):** `underwater.flow(loc, depth)` is the current now (the last cast's seed and time in the water, 20 s on); `rope.st.flow` uses it on every point in the water (`rope.js` replaces `flowX`), so the line's upper part, where the water is faster, bows downstream of the lure. Waiting and not reeling, a lure near the bottom rises to `1.8 × |drift|` px above it (at most 8 px, at 1.5 px/s): about 1.2–1.75 m in the creek, nothing to see in the lakes. The lure's own drift still comes only from `loc.drift`, as before.
113. **The lure's body (22.3):** `underwater.bodyStep` keeps the lure's heading and flutter from its velocity relative to the water: nose first when it moves faster than 2 px/s through the water (sinking, reeled, flying), nose upstream with a 9° downward tilt in a current over 0.05 px/s, toward the boat in still water; turning at 6/s. Each bait is a shape test in the lure's own frame, baked lazily for 16 headings × 2 flutter phases in a 12 px square (`lureImg`, cached by a number, not a string): the worm undulates, the cricket kicks its legs back now and then, the minnow wobbles and flashes a pale pixel, the unlabelled bait's eye turns red. Facing left a bait is mirrored, not turned upside down; only its lower edge takes its dark key. `?gallery` has a `baits` page. The old `lure` tiny sprite is no longer drawn.
114. **The water moves together (22.4):** the weeds lean the flow's way (frame sequence 2, 2, 1, 2, or mirrored; still water below 0.03 px/s sways as before), the motes drift with the flow at their depth, the creek's streaks move at four times it, and the bubbles (`fx.setFlow`) at the flow 2 m down.
115. **Spots by drift (22.5):** the fish AI already reads the drifted lure (`lureIn.x = cast.x1`). A spot's boost was only counted where the lure landed; now a lure the current carries into a spot gains it (`driftIntoSpots`, once per spot per cast, `cast.spotsIn` one bit per spot), and keeps what it had until reeled in. `--replay`, `--pacing` and `--passive` did not change: their player models never drift the lure.
116. **The hook hold (23.1, R2):** `fight.H` 0..1 is set at the hook-set by `bite.hold(t, auto, data)`: 1 − 0.3 × (t / 2 s) from how long the bite had waited (1 at once, `holdLate` 0.7 at the window's end), `holdPassive` 0.85 for passive mode; it never recovers. `fight.start(..., hold)` takes it (1 if left out). The game passes the bite's time, passive mode 0.85, the balance session the time its model player took (0.5 s: 0.925).
117. **Shakes and jumps (23.2, 23.3):** while it pulls, a fish shakes its head in bursts of `shakeLen0..1` (0.4–1 s) every `shakeGap0..1` (1.2–3 s) ÷ its speed. In a shake, T under `slackT` (0.12) drains H at `holdLoss` (0.7/s); reeling in one adds `shakeRise` (0.25/s) of T. A fish with `jumps` (R5: the three pikes 0.6, the muskie 0.8, the sleeper 0.4, the ash trout 0.4, the grandmother 0.3, the dreamer 0.2) leaps with chance `jumps × jumpRate` (0.25) per s while pulling at D < 0.5, for `jumpLen` 1 s: above `jumpHigh` 0.6 T rises a further 0.6/s (it can slip), under `slackT` H drains at `jumpLoss` 0.6/s (a first try at 1.2/s threw every fish on one slack jump). In `tired` there are no shakes or jumps. At H = 0 the fight returns ESCAPE with `thrown` set. The slip, the snap and the 3 s run-out are unchanged (the rule tests).
118. **The auto-player (23.4):** while the fish pulls it keeps `keepT × min(1, keepK × skill)` of tension (0.25, 1.3), and in a shake or jump, once noticed ((1 − skill) × 0.2–1.2 s), the full `keepT`. Its error rate went from 1.5 to 1.0 spells per s × (1 − skill): at 1.5, skill 0.5 did the wrong thing about 70% of the time and fought like random tapping, so no setting put it at 65–80% while tapping stayed at 70% or less. Constants chosen by a sweep (holdLoss 0.5–0.9, slackT 0.12–0.15, keepT, keepK, errRate 0.8–1.2) for the middle of every band.
119. **`--fight` (23.4):** `node tools/balance.js --fight` fights every species each gear tier's rod can hold, 60 times each, by always reeling, never reeling, tapping (holding and letting go for random 0.1–0.5 s spans: a per-frame coin flip averages out to half-reeling, which no hand does), and the auto-player at 0.2, 0.5, 0.8 and 0.85, and prints landed, snapped, thrown and run-out shares per tier with the targets over all tiers. It is a committed check (8.4 #10 in part).
120. **Retune (23.4):** passive went to 56.8% of active, so `passive.incomeMult` is 0.63 (was 0.6; D5, still the one knob): 59.2%. `--pacing` now runs 20 seeds (11, 22, …, 220) instead of 5: over 5 the creek's median moved 7 minutes whenever the random stream was reshuffled (46.1 before this phase, 53.5 after, while 20 seeds give 51.5 before and 46.3 after); the five old seeds are among the twenty. In the model's first hour the player lands more fish than before (284 fights at 97.9% against 266 at 99.6%, shorter fights), so the change itself does not slow the game.
121. **What a loss looks like (23.5):** a shake knocks the rod tip and jolts the line near the fish (less when it is taut); a jump lifts the fish clear of the water in an arc with a splash and a surface dent out and back in (`hook.air`); a thrown hook is the escape the game already had: the line goes loose with the empty lure, the fish swims off, no card, no text, no sting (the soft `lost` splash). The rope straightens from T 0.45 so it is straight by 0.5. `dbg.fight()` and the overlay's `fight` line show T, D, S, mode, H, shake, jump and slips.
122. **Pitch and heave (24.1):** `boat.motion(b, move, dt, hBow, hStern)` runs after `boat.step` (whose speed rules are unchanged, so casting still unlocks at the same moment). Pitch is a spring (K 30/s², C 2/s) toward `2° per 70 px/s²` of forward acceleration plus 1° at full speed and throttle; on a stop the bow drops and it rocks back. The surface's slope under the bow and stern (±15 px) tips it by 60% of that slope (a hull spans the waves; at 100% the swell alone rocked it 2.5°), and its mean height under the hull drives a heave spring (40, 5) that replaces the 1 px sine `bob`. Braking also squats the hull (0.006 px per px/s²). The fisher leans back on throttle (up to 1 px).
123. **Drawn as a shear (24.1):** a rotated painting would resample its pixels (8.3 #84), so the pitch is a shear: the hull is drawn in 2-view-px columns, each moved by `shear(b, dx)` (whole view pixels), and the fisher, the dog, the lantern and the motor each ride the shear at their place; the rod's base rides it and the rod tilts by the pitch. `rodTip` includes both, so the line's anchor is exactly the drawn tip (0.000 view px apart in the check). The mirror pass draws the same.
124. **The boat's own wake (24.2):** the surface's ripples from the stern are too small and too damped at the hull to rock it (0.08 px of heave at most, at any damping tried), so the wake is modelled directly: when the boat drops from over 60% to under 30% of full speed, a following wave reaches it `wakeAfter` 1.3 s later, kicks the heave up and the bow down in proportion to the speed it had, pokes the surface and opens a ring. On flat water the heave then shows the braking squat at 0.55 s and the wake's bump at 2.30 s. Under throttle the propeller churns bubbles and spray under the stern and the bow pushes foam at speed (the `fx` pool never dropped a particle).
125. **The outboard (24.3):** `b.throttle` eases toward the input (up 1/0.6 s, down 1/1.2 s); rpm follows it (idle 900, full 4,500). It starts off: the first move pulls the cord (`b.starts`, 0.4 s cranking) and then runs; after 8 s stopped with nothing held it cuts out, and moving again pulls it once more. The boat's speed does not wait for the motor (step() is unchanged), so for the first 0.4 s it moves while the motor catches. The motor's leg swings back with thrust (its lower half shifted up to 1.5 px), a hard start puffs exhaust, and the dog's ears stream back in the wind above 30% of full speed (`dog.draw(..., wind)`; it is never startled).
126. **Motor and hull sound (24.4):** `makeMotor(ctx, noise, dest)` builds the outboard in any audio context: a pulse train (31 harmonics at 1/√n) at rpm/60 with ±45-cent wander from low-passed noise, through the cowling (430 Hz band-pass), the exhaust (1,150 Hz) and an under-water bubbling (170 Hz low-pass), muffled by a low-pass that opens with the throttle (600–3,200 Hz); gain 0.012 at idle to 0.04 at full, on the ambience bus (so it is muffled indoors). `pullStart` is the cord's zip and three catching thumps. The hull: `slap` (low-passed noise) when the pitch swings back past 0.6° or the wake arrives, at most every 0.3 s; `creak` (two faint triangle tones) now and then past 1.2°, at most every 4 s. `dbg.audio().motor` shows the motor's gain and rpm.
127. **The sound kit (25.1):** `buildKit()` in `js/audio/engine.js` makes every buffer at unlock by plain sample arithmetic from fixed seeds (the plan said an `OfflineAudioContext` render; computing the samples directly gives the same buffers at once, without waiting on a promise): splashes in 5 sizes × 4 variants (an impact of low-passed noise, 1–4 Minnaert bubbles at 3.26 kHz ÷ radius rising as they close, 3–15 droplets over 60–400 ms, a fizz), 3 rises, 4 drips, 2 pops, each reel's click × 3 (its own resonances; the Tin Reel's double rattle), 8 Karplus-Strong line twangs (330–784 Hz, two dampings), 3 nibble ticks, 3 bite knocks, and a reverb impulse per water (1.6 s Fogbound, 1.2 s the Pines, 0.45 s the creek, 4.5 s the Red Room). 85 ms in headless Chromium (68.5 ms in another run). `playBuf` plays within a cap of 28 voices. `dbg.audio().kit` lists the banks.
128. **Splashes (25.2):** a splash's size picks the bank's row and its gain (0.02 + 0.07 × size) times distance. The lure going in: (0.08 + 0.35 × cast distance / 120) × the bait's weight (worm 0.5, cricket 0.75, minnow 1, unlabelled 1.1). A rise is a slurp (`cue('rise')`); a jump is a 0.6 splash out and the biggest, 1.0, back in; a lure lifted out drips (four drips over a second); the landing splashes 0.45 and drips. Played RMS by size: −55.4, −53, −49.9, −48.5, −46.7 dB; the largest peak −28.7 dBFS before the limiter.
129. **Line and reel (25.3):** charging opens the bail (a click in the reel's voice), the release whooshes (keener with the charge), the line hisses off the spool while the lure flies (louder and higher with its speed), and the bail clacks shut as it lands. Reeling turns the handle (2.2 turns/s on an empty lure or a tired fish, 0.7 against a pulling one): a gear whir at 26 × turns Hz through the reel's band and 6 anti-reverse clicks a turn, lighter on a slack line (the rope's `taut`). The drag clicks at 500 × dD/dt per s (at most 90), higher with speed, with a buzz above 45. The tiers: Tin rattles (3.2/4.7/1.9 kHz, a double click), Click clicks cleanly (2.4 kHz), Brass rings (1.65 kHz, long decay), Quiet is near silent, the Reel That Hums hums (a 220 Hz whir). Offline renders of each reel's clicks and whir have spectral centroids of 4,295, 5,428, 3,587, 4,211 and 3,899 Hz. `dbg.audio().reel` shows tier, turns, click rate, whir Hz, drag rate and load.
130. **Biting and fighting (25.4):** a nibble ticks up the rod (`cue('nibble')`, with the rope's twitch); a bite is a knock and two ticks of the rod tip, with a pop when the lure is shallower than 2 m; the hook-set is a twang and a rod sweep. The tension tone is now a thin singing line (a sine with a 5.5 Hz waver at 260 + 520 T Hz), silent when slack (T under 0.12), with a rod creak above T 0.75 every 2.5 s at most. A head shake is three twangs, a slip a quick ratchet (10 clicks in 0.25 s), a snap one high twang and nothing after, a lost or thrown fish a small splash and the empty lure ticking back. A fish pulling above 4 m thrashes at the surface (small splashes, more often the faster it is). The old `plip`, `slip` and splash sounds are replaced.
131. **Space (25.5):** splashes, rises, drips and pops go to `bus.water`, which passes a low-pass running from 18 kHz at the surface to 600 Hz under water (`18000 × (600/18000)^uw.alpha`) into the effects bus and into a convolver with this water's reverb (send 0.25); the line and the reel stay on the effects bus, clear. A low bubbling bed comes up under water (gain 0.02 × alpha, ambience bus). Splashes are panned by the camera and quieter with distance from the boat (1 / (1 + |dx| / 150)). Scenes reach the engine for what state does not show through `G.audio.engine.cue(name, x, a)`.
132. **The mix (25.6):** every new layer goes through the master (mute and volume reach it: muted, master 0). The meter, `G.audio.engine.meter('start' | 'read' | 'stop')`, is two analysers on the master, before and after the limiter, peak and 50 ms RMS. Over a 5-minute scripted session (16 rounds of sailing, stopping and casting, 8 fights) the master peaked at −22.1 dBFS before the limiter and −18.7 after it (−1 allowed), and the loudest 50 ms window was 2.8 dB above the median (−29.7 against −32.5; 6 allowed), so no event stands out of the ambience.
133. **The wake, simulated (26.1; replaces the scripted wake of #124):** `surface.js` has a third layer, `hw`/`vw`, a wave equation for the waves a moving boat makes. Every step `surface.wake(stern x, speed², vx, dt)` pushes it up and down at the stern, swinging once per `wlen` 30 px travelled (one wave per hull length), with `wakeA` 200 view px/s of push at full speed. Its waves travel at `wc` 84 view px/s, the boat's top speed (42 world px/s), in 2 substeps (wc × dt stays under one column); damping 0.2/s, relaxing 0.3/s. Two first tries failed and are recorded: pokes of a dip with raised flanks every 6 px cancelled each other out (a few hundredths of a pixel at the hull), and waves slower than the boat (25, then 38 world px/s) never caught it. With waves at the boat's speed a real stern wave builds behind the hull at speed and rolls under it when the boat brakes. It is part of `at()`, so the waterline, the mirror (it breaks the reflection behind a moving boat), the caustics, the floats and the boat's heave and pitch all feel it. On flat water a stop from full speed heaves +0.18 px at 0.55 s, dips −0.23 at 1.10 and lifts +0.25 at 1.77 as the wake arrives (1.22 s after the first bump). The scripted kick (`wakeAfter`, `wakeLift`, `wakeTip`) is gone; `b.wakeHit` now marks the water lifting the hull faster than 0.6 px/s (the slap and a ring). Driven for 10 simulated minutes, the surface step averages 0.046 ms (0.018 without the wake), no NaN, heights within ±3.
134. **The motor before the boat (26.2; replaces part of #125):** the outboard's logic is now `boat.outboard(b, move, dt)`, called before `boat.step`, and `step` pushes the boat only while `b.motor` runs; during the 0.4 s pull-start a held direction turns the boat (facing) and it drifts as with nothing held. From cold the boat stays at 0.22 px/s (under the 2.5 px/s stopped threshold) until the motor runs at 0.42 s, then reaches 19.1 px/s 0.25 s later; with the motor already running there is no delay (1.9 px/s after one frame, 24 after 20). Casting is unaffected: it needs the boat stopped, which it is during a pull-start.
135. **Every lake cast drifts (26.3; replaces the lakes' direction rule of #111):** `G.data.lakeDrift` now sets each cast's direction from its seed (a bit of the hashed seed: left or right, half each) and sways its strength by ±`sway` (0.2) over `period`; it never passes through zero. `turns: true` keeps the old turning current for the Red Room, on purpose. Fogbound: speed 0.25 + 0.1 × wind; the Pines: 0.26 + 0.08 × wind. Over 200 seeds at 6 m for 30 s every cast drifts 3–10 px at wind 0, 0.5 and 1 (Fogbound 4.2–8.8 px, the Pines 4.6–8.9), and the same seed gives the same path. The creek is unchanged.






87. **Bakes (17):** the shop room takes about 90 ms (at the travel fade's black), each cabin stage about 45 ms, the journal's book about 90 ms on its first opening (one long frame, once per session).
136. **A side-view tail beat (27.1; replaces Phase 19's tail flick):** seen from the side a beating tail sweeps toward and away from the viewer, so `paintLook` foreshortens it instead of dropping it. Frames (`fishgen.FR`): 0 rest; 1 away (the tail 0.55 of its length, 0.92 of its spread, the far side in shadow: fill `finDark`, rays one shade darker, rim `Fn`); 2 the glitch shimmer, unchanged; 3 near (0.75 of its length, 1.12 of its spread, lit). A beat runs `fishgen.BEAT` = rest, away, rest, near. Frame indices 0 and 1 kept their numbers, so older code that alternated `& 1` still draws a beat. `generate()` (the old sprite rows, used only to validate looks) is unchanged.
137. **Turning frames (27.2):** frames 4 and 5 paint the whole look narrowed across about the canvas's middle to 0.6 and 0.3 of its length (`wx`/`PX` map through the squash; painted that way, never a scaled sprite), so a fish seen at an angle as it turns keeps its outline, eye and fins at full pixel size. Every frame keeps the canvas size, centre and `midY`; the mouth moves with the squash and `mouthX` carries it. A reversal plays TURN1, TURN2, then TURN2 and TURN1 flipped, over `0.2 + 0.006 × length` s (0.28–0.37 s for the roster), capped at 0.4; a turn asked to go back the way it came unwinds from where it is.
138. **The swimming body (27.3):** `underwater.swimStep(run, dt)` runs in `sailing.update` before `ropeStep` and steps nine bodies (the school's eight slots and the hooked fish), each keyed by the fish's `uid` so a reused slot starts fresh. A body smooths the fish's velocity from its positions (0.25 a step), so it never reads or writes sim state. Beat rate: `0.7 + 1.4 × speed / body length` Hz, capped at 6 (measured 0.70–3.03 Hz over a 60 s school, median 1.07); a fish slowing harder than 2 px/s² glides: its tail finishes the stroke and rests. Facing changes only past 1.5 px/s of `vx` (below it, the sim's `dir`), always through a turn. Pitch: the slope `vy / speed` (±0.42, about 23°), scaled down under 3 px/s and level during a turn, followed at 4/s. `drawSwimmer` draws a pitched swimmer in vertical strips each `round(1 / |slope|)` view px wide (2–8), each dropped by the slope at its middle, and in one piece when the drop across the body is under 1.5 view px. The wrongness effects keep their hashes: the out-of-step beat now offsets the phase by up to 0.225 × w, the backward beat flips the drawn facing, and the shimmer replaces the frame outside turns.
139. **Every swimmer through it (27.4):** the hooked fish's body takes its facing from `hook.face` alone (its thrash is in its position), its beat from the fight (4.5 Hz pulling, 1 Hz tired, 3 Hz while lifted), its pitch from its motion; its ±1 px thrash jitter stays until Phase 29. `mouth()` reads the same frame, facing and strip drop that the draw uses, so the rope ties on to the drawn mouth through turns (0.000 px off in every step of three fights). The far silhouettes beat at 1 Hz and the heavy shadow at 0.6 Hz with the four-frame beat.
140. **Swimmer bake (27.1):** each species is baked in 5 frames (6 with the shimmer) × colour/silhouette × both facings at the travel fade, instead of 2–3. To pay for it a silhouette is now the colour frame's shape filled flat with `water_deep` (`source-in`), the same pixels painting it gave (0 of 159,468 pixels differ in shape), so each frame is painted once. A water's first load: the creek 131 ms (146 before), the Pines 301 (264), the Red Room 115 (101).
141. **Heading and speed (28.1):** a school fish has `yaw` (0 swimming right, π left; in between it swims across the view, toward or away from the viewer, so its horizontal speed passes through zero as it turns, as a side view shows a real turn), `pitch` (at most asin 0.6, so vertical speed is never over 0.6 of its speed) and `spd`, set in `fish_ai.M`. Each state asks for a ground velocity and `swim()` steers toward it: yaw at `2.4 + 1 × species speed` rad/s (a reversal 0.58 s or more, measured), pitch at 1.6 rad/s, speed up at 1.5 × top speed per s, down at 3/s. `vx`/`vy` stay the sim's outputs, so the bite, the hook-set and the scene read them as before. `face` is the heading's side; the swimming body (27.3) now takes its facing from it and its beat, glide and pitch from the sim (`swimOne(..., sim)`), not from the ground speed, which the current adds to. The V3.4 states, their order, their timers and every `rng` draw are unchanged.
142. **Burst and glide (28.2):** wandering and approaching fish burst toward 1.45 × the speed their state wants for 0.4–1.0 s, then glide (drag 0.9/s) until under 0.55 × it or 2 s, then burst again; a cycle's median is 1.78 s. Burst lengths come from a hash of the fish's `uid` and its burst count (`hfrac`), not from the rng, so the bursts add nothing to the random stream. Fleeing and circling fish swim steadily. Over 20 seeds × 4 waters × 5 min against a still lure (Node, `fish_ai` alone): approach 3.21 s on average (3.36 before, −4%), circling 22.75 s (22.70), spawn to bite 46.3 s (49.3), 4.30 bites a minute (4.08, +5%).
143. **Current and holding station (28.3):** `school.ctx.flowAt(x, depth)` (the scene sets it to `underwater.flow` on waters with `loc.drift`; `null` in the balance models, which have no current) carries every fish. A wandering fish not heading for the lure faces into a current over 0.5 px/s: it swims upstream, or slips back tail first no faster over the bed than 0.8 of the current, so it never turns to swim with it. A fish heading for the lure (within scent and its `dir` toward it) swims after it either way: holding station for every wanderer stalled the fish behind a drifting lure and cut creek bites to 0.98 a minute. Against a lure drifting at 0.8 of a 3 px/s current, 3.65 bites a minute (3.77 before); fish not heading for the lure face upstream 98% of the time (51% before).
144. **Circling and nibbling by steering (28.4):** circling steers after a point 0.35 rad ahead on the same flattened circle (`circleK` 2.5/s closes the gap) at most 0.8 of top speed; the circle's angle advances as before, so circling takes as long (22.75 s against 22.70). Nibbling is `hover()`: the fish faces the lure and darts in to 2 px for the first 40% of each nibble, then backs off to 7 px, its velocity set straight (`dartK` 9/s, vertical at most 0.6 of top speed); backing is kept in `back`, which dies away at 4/s once it swims again, so leaving after a nibble is not a sudden reversal. Biting holds 4 px from the lure.
145. **Baselines moved (28):** `--replay 12345` is `82118876` (18 catches, 19 casts). `--pacing` over the 20 seeds: first upgrade 6.0, creek 48.7 min, red 325.6 min, all inside their bands. The creek's median rose from 40.4 by luck of the reshuffled stream, not slower fishing: over 80 other seeds (11 i + 5000) it is 51.1 min now against 54.2 with the old code, and in the model's first 40 minutes the player lands 727 fish against 700, with the same species mix. `--passive` 62.4% (59.2%); time away is unchanged (passive does not use the school). `--fight` and `--saves` unchanged.
146. **Runs (29.1):** while pulling, the hooked fish's path (`hookPos`) no longer shakes ±3 px across and ±2 px down at about 2 Hz, and the draw's ±1 px jitter is gone (only the wrongness twitch stays). Instead it bores in slow arcs (`underwater.HOOK`: 1.5 px across, 4 px down, a period of about 1.8 s) and dives `10 × stamina` px deeper than its line depth while pulling, so it rises when it tires. Its body keeps facing away from the boat pulling and toward it tired, through turns; its beat is `2.5 + 3.5 × stamina` Hz pulling and `0.6 + 0.8 × stamina` tired (3 while lifted out). In four scripted fights (auto-player skill 0.85) its facing matched its horizontal motion in 89–96% of pull steps where it moved over 2 px/s (the rest are a pull's first moments, while the fish still coasts toward the boat), and its pitch matched its vertical motion in 97–99%.
147. **Head shakes (29.2):** frame 7 (`FR.SHAKE`) paints the head from the gill (`HEAD_X` 0.62) forward foreshortened to 0.7, the head turned. While `fight.shake` runs, the body alternates REST and SHAKE at 8 per second and its head jerks up and down by a slope of ±0.16 (`jerk`, added to the pitch the draw and `mouth()` use). Every shake step outside a turn drew the shake (1,222 steps over four fights) and no SHAKE frame appeared outside one.
148. **The roll (29.2):** frame 6 (`FR.ROLL`) paints the fish listed onto its side: the back narrowed, the belly band widened with a pale flash (`Lb` one shade lighter), the dorsal fins foreshortened to 0.55 and the belly fins at 1.3, the tail to 0.85. A tired fish with T under 0.35 and stamina under 0.5 shows it for 0.7 s of every 2.2 s of fight time; it never showed outside that rule (618 roll frames over four fights). Frames 6 and 7 are only ever hooked, so each species bakes them in colour only (4 more canvases); a water's first load measured 134 ms (the creek), 122–126 (the Pines) and 59–69 (the Red Room) on a quiet machine.
149. **A cut-short jump falls back (29.1):** when a jump ended early (the fight turning tired mid-leap) the old clamp snapped the fish from up to 6 px in the air to 2 px under water in one step. It now falls at the jump's rate (14/s) until it is back in, splashing as it enters. Outside jumps and the step that ends one, the hooked fish moved at most 0.8 px a step.
150. **Far shoals (30.1; replace the four lone far silhouettes):** three shoals (perch, trout, carp), 4–7 fish each (carp 3–4), in the far layer (parallax 0.5, wrapping every 640 px). Each fish steers by its shoal (`underwater.SB`): separation within 1.3 × length or 1.4 × height (weight 3/s), alignment (1.2/s), cohesion (0.35/s, ×3 beyond 60 px) and the shoal's cruise (0.8/s): 2–5 px/s one way, turning round every 10–25 s, at a depth that drifts by up to 10 px each turn. A hard spacing rule (three passes of position pushes along the cheaper axis) keeps any two of a shoal from being closer than 0.85 × length across while within 0.7 × height down. Speeds are capped at 8 px/s, vertical at 0.6 of speed. Their beat runs at `1 + speed / 4` Hz; facing changes past 1 px/s through 0.24 s of the TURN2 frame. They keep above half the water's deepest point (`SB.deep`): the near bed is drawn over the far layer, so the old full-depth range hid them behind it. Over 60 s: 0 overlaps in 3,600 steps, the widest shoal's spread median 18.9 px, at most 23.5.
151. **Startle (30.2):** `fx.listen(fn)` (new) calls back on every `fx.splash`; the shoals map its world x to the far layer at the camera (`x − camX × 0.5 + 160`) and startle every fish within `30 + 60 × power` px (vertical distance counted at 0.7). The boat moving faster than 6 px/s startles those within 40 px of it. A startled fish whips round through the turn frame, flees away from the source and a little down at up to 22 px/s (reached at 12/s), fading over 1.6 s, then its shoal pulls it back. A splash of power 1 on a shoal's fish: all 5 within reach faced away at once (1 step) and were over 1.5 × their speed in 0.03–0.05 s; 6 s later the widest shoal spanned 26.9 px, still closing; the boat driven through at 40 px/s startled 5.
152. **Cost:** at most 21 far fish (13 in the checked water): fish draw and under-water draw 0.1 ms; bakes for the shoals' frames (REST, AWAY, NEAR, TURN2, both ways, silhouettes) at the water's load. 59.9 fps.
153. **The rod as a beam (31.1, new file like R1):** `js/gfx/rod.js` (`G.gfx.rod`, loaded after `rope.js`) is 12 segments of 22/12 world px from the hands, the first two rigid (fore grip, reel seat). Joint i's compliance is `seg / d⁴` with the diameter thinning linearly from 1 at the butt to the tier's taper at the tip (`K.taper` 0.5, 0.42, 0.35, 0.3, 0.25 for rod tiers 0–4: slow to fast action), normalised so `Σ cᵢ (L − sᵢ)² = K.power · L` (1.1): every tier moves its tip alike under a small load (0.724–0.725 px at load 0.03, all five tiers) and differs only in where it bends. A tip force (the load 0..1 of the rod's rating times the line's unit direction) bends each joint by `cᵢ × (tip − jointᵢ) × F`, 6 relaxed passes (0.5) per step from the last shape, so the solve carries over from step to step. Because the moment about a joint falls to zero when the blank beyond it lines up with the force, a heavy load turns the tip toward the line by itself. Local frame x forward, y up; `tip(f, bx, by, out)` gives it in world px. Typed arrays, nothing allocated per step, no randomness.
154. **The load from the line (31.2):** `rodPose` gives `[angle, load, pull]`: the hands' angle as before, except the fight holds the rod higher (`waitAngle + 18 − 8T`, it was `+ 8 − 10T`), since the bend now brings the tip down; the load by state (line weight 0.03 sinking and waiting; the bite 0.15–0.35 in nods with the hands' tilt cut from 9° to 4°; pulling `0.3 + 0.7T`, tired `0.1 + 0.3T`; the hook-set +0.5 fading over 0.3 s; head shakes +0.15; landing 0.35; passive 0.03, or 0.45 ± 0.15 with a fish on; 0.02 hanging straight down between casts and on the card); and where it pulls: along the line (from the tip to rope point 3 of the last step), straight down, or (charging and casting, until Phase 32) across the rod toward its back, the flick bending it back up to 35°. `rodStep` runs in `sailing.update` after the boat's motion and before `ropeStep`. `boat.rodTip(b, cx, wy, out)` (it took an angle and gave the straight rod's tip) now gives the bent tip; the rope's point 0, the lure riding the tip during the flick, where the flight starts and `drawCast` all read it, and `boat.rodBase` gives the hands.
155. **The rod's parts (31.3):** drawn in view pixels along the bent blank, two keys per part, nothing rotated (8.3 #84): a cork butt 4.5 px behind the hands along their angle with an ink cap (`wood_lit`/`wood_mid`), the fore grip, a reel seat (`stone_hi`/`stone_dark`), the blank two pixels across its lower half (the second pixel on the underside, across whichever axis the blank is nearer to crossing, so it keeps its thickness standing upright) in the tier's key over a shade key (`ROD_SHADE`), one pixel above; five guides at 0.22–0.88 of the length with rings 3, 2, 2, 1, 1 view px under the blank and an ink wrap; a `stone_lit` tip-top; a reel hanging 4 px under the seat with a handle. The line runs from the reel's spool through the rings to the tip in straight chords, drawn under the blank in the colour and alpha of the line out (`fog_light` 0.6, the tension key 0.85 in a fight). `boat.draw` no longer takes an angle and a bend; it reads `G.gfx.rod` and `gear.lineKey`/`gear.lineA`.
156. **Debug (31.4):** `dbg.rodBody()` returns `{load, dir, tip, bendDeg, top3, butt, tier, taper, ms}`; `dbg.rodLoad(x)` forces the load (`null` gives it back); an overlay line `rod tier … load … bend … top3 … butt …`. `dbg.rodPose()` returns `[angle, load, pull]`.
157. **Cost:** a solve is 0.021 ms in Node (1,000 random solves after warm-up, 21 ms); the draw is about 120 one-pixel rects.
158. **Why it bent both ways (31b):** measured through whole casts in the browser and over a sweep in Node. (a) #153's relaxation (each joint set to `c × moment` in the shape of the last pass, 6 passes at 0.5) is a fixed-point iteration that, once the blank is soft, swings to wrong shapes: with power 1.6–2.2 and a 0.08–0.12 taper it settled at loads of 0.6–1 on shapes bent **backward** (+12° to +42°) or nearly straight with the line 107–150° off the tip, and the shipped settings were close to that edge (fights reach loads over 1). (b) No inertia: the bite's nods (`|sin 12t|`), mode changes and the hook-set moved the tip up to 4 px in a step, so it flicked forward and straight several times a second. (c) The tip did not follow the line: at moderate loads it pointed 17° up with the line leaving 56° down, a 70° corner. (d) The load ignored the line: 0.73 on the rod with the line sagging 11 px.
159. **The solver (31b, supersedes #153's relaxation):** two shapes. `q`, the settled shape for the present load, by Jacobi relaxation in which each joint's step toward `c × moment` is divided by `1 + c × max(0, lever · F)` (its own Newton term; 6 passes at 0.3, warm-started each step); and `d`, the drawn shape, each joint a damped spring toward `q` at one frequency, the blank's first mode (`K.hz` 3.2, `zeta` 0.3, taken implicitly in 4 substeps). A blend of settled curves is one smooth curve, so the rod never ripples into an S while it moves (each joint chasing its own target let the soft fast tip run past the line ahead of the stiff butt and come back: a 13.6° counter-bent arm on a sudden load). Released from full load it swings past straight twice and is within 5% in 0.28–0.33 s.
160. **The line's stretch (31b):** the load and the line's direction reach the blank through a 0.09 s lag (`K.stretch`), as a nylon line's stretch spreads a sudden pull; when the boat turns the local direction is taken at once.
161. **A stiffer, more tapered blank (31b, supersedes #153's numbers):** `K.power` 2.0 (1.1) and tip/butt 0.32, 0.26, 0.2, 0.15, 0.12 by tier (0.5 … 0.25), nearer real blanks (0.1–0.3), so under load the tip follows the line: with the hands at 58° and the line 50° down, 16° off at load 0.6 on the slowest rod and at most 6.8° at load 1; in a browser fight the tip is within 10–13° of a tight line on average (was 31–35° at the same tensions).
162. **Load only through a tight line (31b):** `rodStep` scales a pull (any load over 0.05) by `1 / (1 + q²)`, `q = sag / (1 + 0.05 × span)`, never under the line's weight 0.02: a line that sags lets the rod spring back. The fight's load is the tension in both modes, `0.12 + 0.85 T` (+0.08 while the fish runs), and so is its slack (supersedes #154 and 21's rule): straight from T 0.45 whatever the mode, sagging `0.14 × (0.5 − T)` under it (+1% and up to 2 px for a tired fish), +15% for 0.35 s after a slip; before, a tired fish's line sagged 6–8% at T 0.6–0.85. The landing loads 0.55 (0.35): the fish's weight hangs from the tip.
163. **Waiting, nibbles and the bite (31b):** waiting with nothing on, the angler takes in the slack the sink left (the line out eases toward `1.03 × span + 1.5` at 0.9/s; a lure that drifts away still draws line out): over 120 px of line the sag falls from 13 to about 7 px in 5 s, and the tip droops a few degrees under the line's weight. A nibble ticks the tip (+0.07 for 0.12 s) as it twitches the line. A bite takes the slack out over about a third of a second (`e^(−7t)`, the straightening `pull` rising to 0.3 over 0.3 s; it was instant), then tugs: 2.6 a second, sharp in and slower out, `0.1 + min(0.15, 0.2t) + 0.3 × pulse`, while the hands keep still (the 4° tilt is gone). The tip moves about 8 px on a bite (P1-4 asks for 2). The hook-set sweeps the rod up 10° and back over 0.3 s with its surge of load.
164. **The rod in front of the dog (31b, closes 8.4 #27):** it reaches out over the dog in the bow, so `boat.draw` draws it after him and the whole bend shows.
165. **Hands that move like hands (32):** `rod.solve` no longer takes the scene's angle as it is: the hands follow it critically damped at 2.5 Hz (`K.handsHz`), so a state change (40° waiting to 58° hooked, 70° landing to 50° on the card) is a movement, never a jump; with `direct` (the charge and the cast) they follow the scene's angle exactly. Outside the cast and the strike they move at most 0.48° a step in the browser checks; the strike (an 18° lift plus the 10° sweep) peaks at 122°/s, a real hook-set's speed. The hands' angular speed and acceleration are kept (`st.hv`, `st.al`).
166. **The cast loads the rod (32.2, replaces 31's ROD_BACK stand-in):** the hands' angular acceleration α is an inertial load on the blank's own mass (m ∝ d²) and, while it hangs at the tip, the lure's (`K.lure` 3 px of butt): joint i's spring aims at `qᵢ − K.inertia × α × cᵢ × (Iwᵢ + IwLᵢ)`, where `Iwᵢ = Σ m s (s − sᵢ)` beyond the joint (`K.inertia` 0.9e-5). That bend has one sign along the blank, so a swing bends it in one curve too. The stroke is eased `u²(2 − u)` over `CAST.flick` (it gathers speed and ends at full speed); the rod coming up to wait is eased by a half cosine, so the stop is the hard deceleration that fires the rod. Once the lure flies only the blank's own mass whips it (with the lure counted after the stop the forward whip reached 95°; it is 28–50° now, from a light to a full charge). In Node: the tip trails the straight rod by 6.2–7.5 px on a full stroke, whips 5.2–6.7 px past straight and springs back 10–13° past the bend it comes to rest at (about 20° forward, under the line and the rising hands). In the browser: trails 8.2 px and whips 6.2 px past (charge 0.8), 3.7 and 4.0 (charge 0.2); the lure leaves from the drawn tip (0.000 px) and lands on the marker.
167. **The line rides the tip until the lure flies (32):** during the stroke the rope is held collapsed at the tip every step. The whipping tip moves too fast for 48 links, solved 8 times, to follow, and they trailed behind it in a loop much longer than the line out.
168. **Head shakes as impulses (32.1):** `rod.kick(rad/s)` adds speed to the drawn joints, more toward the tip, the way the line pulls the settled shape; each head-shake twitch of the line (every 0.12 s) kicks the rod 3–5 rad/s (`SHAKE_KICK` 5 × 0.6–1). The hands no longer dip and the load no longer rises with `|sin 26t|`. The tip moves up to 2.4–2.6 px in a step during shakes.
169. **The bite through the tip (32.3):** 31b's tugs already satisfy it: the tip moves 7.7–9.8 px on a bite while the hands turn 0.10–0.17° (the task allows 2°).
170. **The reel (32.4):** `rod.reel(dt, turns, open)` keeps the handle's turn (`st.crank`) and the bail (`st.bail`). The handle turns `10 × reelSpeed` a second (1.2 on the Tin Reel, 3.0 on the Reel That Hums) while the action key reels a lure that is sinking, waiting or interested (not with a fish on the bite: then it hooks), and in the fight while reeling (`underwater.fightReel()`, new) at `1 − 0.5T` of that, never while the drag gives line (`fight.slipped`); half speed while passive mode fights a fish. The bail is open from the charge through the sink, unless reeling closes it: a sinking lure runs line off an open bail, as 21's rope already modelled, so it closes when the lure stops or reeling starts rather than as the lure lands. Drawn in view pixels: a `bone` knob on an ink arm going round in 8 steps, the way a crank turns for the facing; the bail a `stone_lit` wire across the spool's face, or thrown back toward the blank when open. The reel is drawn after the hull (`drawReel`): the hands sit at the gunwale, and the hull hid the reel that hangs under them (Phase 31's too).
171. **`--rod` gains the cast (32):** a 14th check runs the stroke as sailing.js does at every tier. In the browser (a scratchpad script, three seeds) the handle turned on 159–228 steps and never while not reeling, the bail was never wrong, and no step was an S.
172. **A posable fisher (33.1, new file):** `js/scene/fisher.js` (`G.scene.fisher`, loaded after `boat.js`) replaces `boat.js`'s fisher art and its `sip` variant. Layers in the view's pixels, never rotated: the far arm (darker, behind), the body, the near arm, the collar, the head (tilt 0 ahead, 1 down, 2 back: the face's pixels and the brim move a pixel), and the near arm after the head when the hand is above the shoulder line (at the mouth). Each arm runs from its shoulder (image px 12,15 near, 8,15 far) through an elbow found by two-bone IK (5 and 4.6 px, the elbow hanging low) to the hand, painted with `paint.limb` and the hand with `paint.blob`, cached per half view pixel (102 arm canvases after a cast, two acts, a bite and a fight). The rod pose keeps Phase 18's painted elbow; in it the new figure differs from the old painting in 6 pixels of 520, all the far hand, which now shows on the rod. Local frame: x forward from the boat's centre, y down from the seat line; the left-facing fisher is drawn 1 world px further left than before so both facings share it.
173. **Poses and easing (33.2):** a pose is the near and far hands' places (`rod`, `knees`, `holder`, `cup`, `mouth`); each coordinate is a critically damped spring at 12 rad/s, 32 going back to the rod. The rod's base is the near hand (it was `ROD.baseX/baseY`, now the `rod` pose's hand, 0.25 px further forward), or the holder; `boat.rodBase` reads `fisher.rodLocal`. In the browser the hands moved at most 0.83 px a step and the rod's base 0.62 px.
174. **The rod holder (33.3):** a tube on the gunwale behind his hands (local −1, −4.5), drawn over the hull; in it the rod stands at 38° and still bends with the line: a bite bent it to −12° to −17° there. The hand carries the rod to the holder before it lets go (`holder` pose, 0.5 s) and comes back for it.
175. **The idle director (33.4):** `fisher.step(fs, dt, {mode, input}, G.vrng)` from `sailing.fisherStep` (before `rodStep`). Mode `casts`: the sailing state, stopped, not passive, no anomaly; `wait`: the waiting state with no bite, not passive, no anomaly (interest is its own state, so it counts as something happening). After 6–12 s (`casts`) or 8–15 s (`wait`) of the mode with no key held or pressed, it plays an act chosen by `G.vrng`: `thermos` between casts (P2-2's sip, which was a fixed 3 s in every 34 s; rod to the holder, the cup, two drinks with the head back, the rod back: 7.6 s), `rest` while waiting (the rod in the holder, hands on his knees for 7 s). A key, a change of mode or a bite ends an act at once: the item goes, the near hand fetches the rod from the holder and takes it back, in 0.33–0.37 s; the keys act on the same step (the action key mid-act started the charge on the next step sampled). The rod's angle in the holder or in an act overrides `rodPose`'s angle.
176. **Debug (33.5):** `dbg.fisher()` returns `{pose, hands, holder, act, step, actT, idleT, wait, returning, head, item}`; `dbg.idle(name)` starts an act (null ends it). An act started outside its mode ends on the next step.
177. **Plan wording:** 33.1's "within 4 px of painted difference" is read as the rod pose against the old painting (6 px, the far hand); 33.4's "back to the rod within 0.2 s" became 0.4 s, since the hand first fetches the rod from the holder.
178. **The lure hangs from the tip (34.1):** between casts (the sailing, charging and card states, not passive, no line out) and during the cast's stroke the lure hangs on 3 px of line under the rod tip: a pendulum (`sailing.dangleStep`, after `rodStep`) pivoting at the tip, gravity 40 px/s², damped 1.4/s, swung by the tip's acceleration from finite differences. The game's boat starts at about 1.8 g in real units, which flipped the lure over (113°), so it feels 40% of the tip's acceleration (`DG.aK`): it swings up to 52° when the boat starts and stops and settles within 0.2° under the tip. The hanging lure pulls the tip its way (`ROD_DOWN` follows it). The cast's flight starts from where it hangs (`c.x0`), and the stroke's line runs from the tip to it. Drawn as a line and the bait's own sprite (`underwater.drawLure`).
179. **Preparing the lure (34.2):** the act `lure` (between casts, 8.95 s, picked with the thermos by `G.vrng`): the rod stood upright (96°) in the holder; line paid out (14 px/s, the bail open) until the lure hangs before his face (16.4 px); the near hand takes it and holds it (the lure follows the hand exactly, the line straight from the tip); he looks it over (head down), works the bait on in four quick changes of hand, tugs the knot, lets it go to swing, takes the rod back and reels the lure up under the tip (10 px/s, the handle turning 2.2 a second). Act steps gained `line`, `reel`, `bail`, `held`, `tug` and `cue`, and an act its own holder angle.
180. **The holder moved before his knees (34):** to local (3.5, −4.5) from (−1, −4.5), so an upright rod stands in front of his face rather than across it. The thermos and rest acts lean the rod forward from there.
181. **A tug along an upright rod (34):** the line from the tip to his hand runs nearly down the rod, which compresses it rather than bending it, and this soft blank buckles under compression from about 0.2 of its rating (a 0.6 pull folded it to −170°). The tug pulls 0.18 with the hand drawn down and out a little: a 6° nod.
182. **Sounds (34.3):** `cue('bait')` (a light, high tick as the bait goes on, twice) and `cue('hooked')` (a firmer tick and a small pop as the hook is set) from the act's steps; the engine's reel whir and clicks also run while the act reels (`fisher.reel`).
183. **The cooler (35.1):** a 12×9 view px cooler (a pale lid with a handle, a teal body lit on one side) on the floor behind him (local −5, −4), drawn before him and the hull so the gunwale hides its foot; on every water and in the mirror.
184. **The beer (35.2):** three acts in both of the director's modes (acts now list their `modes`; the thermos plays while waiting too). `beer` (8.5 s): the rod to the holder, a can from the cooler, cracked (`cue('crack')` and three foam particles from the can, `fx.emit('splash')`), a drink with the head back, set on the gunwale by his hip (local −2.2, −4.2; drawn after the hull), a rest, the rod back. `sip` (7.2 s): from the can on the gunwale and back. `empty` after three drinks: the last of it, the can back in the cooler, then 90 s before another (`BEER_REST`). One can at a time; never while a fish is on (those states are not the director's modes). Interrupted holding the can, he sets it down on the gunwale at once (`cue('candown')`) and takes the rod; a bite during it was hooked as usual.
185. **The beer and the thermos (35.3):** `fisher.drink(fs, night)`: the thermos in the grey hours (`greyF(hour) ≥ 0.5`, passed as `night` by `sailing.fisherStep`); at dusk and night a beer, a sip or the empty by the can's state, the thermos while the last can is resting. The director picks the drink or the mode's other act (preparing the lure; a rest) half and half, the drink 65% when a can is open at night.
186. **Sounds (35.4):** `cue('crack')` (a high tick and the new `KIT.hiss`: noise with its lows removed by differencing, in at once, fading over about 0.35 s) and `cue('candown')` (the bite's knock pitched up 2.6×, quiet), synthesised in the kit like every other sound (§0.2).
187. **Debug:** `dbg.fisher()` adds `can`, `sips`, `beerRest`, `night` and `drink` (what he would drink now).
188. **The room (36.1, 36.2, new file):** `js/ui/shop_room.js` (`G.ui.shopRoom`, loaded before `shop.js`): a 12 × 8 map of 32 px tiles (384 × 256) at (128, 52), black around it, painted natively in the lake's keys (31 colours, all palette keys; wood in its six keys) and baked once (about 1 ms). North wall: log faces with a dark top, two windows (their glass drawn live; a still dusk until Phase 39), a clock with live hands, a rod rack, a low shelf with the unlabelled jar and a tin, a tall equipment shelf with the lantern and the finder. Side and south walls: their tops, lit along the edge toward the room. An L-shaped counter with a till on the right, Rusty's side behind it (empty until Phase 37); the worm box, the cricket crate and the minnow tank along the west wall; a moss-green rug; the door and its mat at the bottom. 52 tiles block.
189. **The fisher on foot (36.3):** a top-down fisher (the red cap, the green jacket, dark trousers) in four facings, a standing frame and two walking frames each, painted natively (left is right mirrored). Steps tile to tile in 0.2 s (measured 0.200 s over 16 steps); a new direction turns him on the spot and he walks once it has been held 0.09 s; the last direction pressed and still held leads; walls and objects block. He comes in on the mat facing up.
190. **The dog follows (36.4):** the dog top-down (a yellow Lab nearly a tile long: four facings, standing, walking, sitting) takes the tile he leaves, faces where it goes and sits facing him when he stops. In a headless walk of 586 steps neither ever stood on a blocking tile, nor the dog on him.
191. **The bridge (36.5):** `shop.js` has two modes: `room` (walking) and `panel` (the old painted shop and its tabs). Facing the counter (any of its tiles) and pressing Space opens the panel, where buying works as before (`TODO(P37.2)`); Esc or M there goes back to the room; in the room Esc or M, or walking down off the mat through the door, leaves for the lake.
192. **Debug:** `dbg.shop()` returns `{c, r, face, moving, steps, dog, mode, faced, bakeMs, room, blocked}`.
193. **Rusty top-down (37.1):** painted natively in `shop_room.js` (a pale canvas jacket, a white beard, a paper-coloured cap) behind the counter on tile (9, 2), drawn over the room above the counter's top; frames: standing, blinking, a sip from his cup, reading the paper. Alone he reads, sipping for 1.6 s in every 11; spoken to he looks up (down, or left when you stand at the counter's side) and blinks every 4.3 s. The old leaning Rusty, the painted room (walls, pegboards, trophies, sign, cobwebs, lamp, ashtray) and the tabbed panel are removed from `shop.js` (40.3's cleanup, done here).
194. **The text box (37.2):** along the bottom (24, 290, 592 × 62), a cream window in a dark wood frame with a paper rule, the Pokémon way. The visit's line (`pickLine`, still chosen on entering) is typed at 48 characters a second, two lines a page; Space finishes the page, then turns it; a blinking arrow says there is more; a repeated line has its own pages, a reversed one runs backwards in crimson. Then the choice (*BUY*, *TALK*, *LEAVE*; W/S and Space): *TALK* types the line again, *LEAVE* closes. Esc closes the box or the choice; M closes everything; while something is open he does not move.
195. **The item window (37.3):** *BUY* opens a list (336, 14, 280 × 270) of every shelf's items (rods, reels, lines, the lantern, the finder, the four baits) with their icons, names and prices (OWNED, BEST, FREE when it applies; the count in stock and ON for the bait on the hook), the coins top left, and in the box the chosen item's name, price, what it does and its state or the last word ('Thank you kindly.', 'Not enough coins.', 'On the hook next cast.'). W/S choose, Space buys through the same `economy` calls as before; Esc goes back to the choice. Every item could be bought or put on the hook in a headless check.
196. **Text keys:** the box's text is `wood_deep` (`crimson` reversed); messages `moss_dark` for thanks, `rust` for too few coins; prices `rust`, states `stone`.
197. **Debug:** `dbg.shop()` adds `open` ('', 'box', 'choice', 'items'), `page`, `pages`, `choice`, `items`, `sel`, `from` and `rusty` (facing, frame); `mode` is gone with the panel.
198. **Shelves you face (38.1):** facing the rod rack (rods, reels, lines), the jar shelf (the unlabelled jar), the equipment shelf (lantern, depth finder), the worm box, the cricket crate or the minnow tank and pressing Space opens the item window with that shelf's items (`SHELF_ITEMS`); Esc there closes it (there is no choice behind it). Rusty's *Buy* still lists all nine.
199. **What the shelves show (38.2):** drawn live over the baked room: the rack holds the rods above the one he owns (four at the start, one fewer for each upgrade, by the window or `dbg.buy`); the lantern and the finder sit on their shelf until bought; minnows swim to and fro in the tank and crickets hop in the crate. The sim's stock is endless (V8), so the tank and the crate show 7 and 6 less two for each pack bought this visit (never fewer than one), full again on the next visit.
200. **Debug:** `dbg.shop().shown` gives what the shelves showed in the last frame (`rods`, `lantern`, `finder`, `minnows`, `crickets`).
201. **The hour in the windows (39.1):** the glass shows the lake: five sky bands from `sailing.skyColor` (the lake's keys, rebuilt when the hour moves by 0.05), the far shore (`mount_far`, `pine_dark`), the water with a faint band of the sky in it, fog over it at `sailing.fogAlpha()`, and drizzle running down the glass with `sailing.rain()` (Fogbound's). The top band matched `skyColor(h, 0)` exactly at 03:00, 07:00, 12:00 and 19:00; the brightest pixel in the windows (89 at 19:00) stays under `dusk_high` (148).
202. **The light in the room (39.1):** at night (`sailing.light().night`) a mask baked once darkens the room in four steps of 2 px cells (0, 0.18, 0.34, 0.5 of ink) away from a lamp hung over the rug, leaving the window glass alone; the lamp's bulb glows in `lamp_glow`. By day a flat `fog_mid` wash at 0.07 below the windows. At 23:00 the floor under the lamp measured 55 against 30 in the far corner; at noon 56 and 42.
203. **Life (39.2):** nine motes of dust drift in the lamplight at night, three bubbles rise in the tank, Rusty's cup steams on the counter (except while he sips from it), and the clock's hands follow the game hour (at 09:15 the minute hand points at three). All on fixed rhythms of the scene's time.
204. **The strange (39.3):** at Fogbound's unease (`unease.get(save, 'fog')`, the shop's, 8.3 #23) of 0.4 or more, after 20–40 s and then every 40–80 s, one of: the clock's minute hand running back two turns over 3 s, or a pale figure (`fog_mid`; a dark one was lost against the night) standing on the far shore in the right window for 6 s, fading in and out; at 0.6 or more, on entering, half the time Rusty has his back to you (a back view) until you speak to him. Never two at once, never at unease below 0.4. Over 10 simulated minutes: none at unease 0; at unease 1 the clock 6 times, the figure 3, and Rusty's back on 7 of 20 entries. `G.vrng` decides (presentation only).
205. **Sounds (39.4):** the door's bell (two soft sine tones) on entering and leaving the shop, in place of the page sound; a footstep (the bite's knock pitched up, quiet) on each step; the third board from the door (two above the mat) hums low when stepped on, as Rusty's line says; the tank's pump hums at 60 and 120 Hz while in the shop; drizzle on the glass through the indoor low-pass. Synthesised like the rest (§0.2).
206. **Touch (40.1):** the shop needed no new input code: in the shop `input.zone` already gives the left and right edges as left and right, the top and bottom middle as up and down and the centre as the action, so a held touch walks and a tap at the centre talks, turns the box's pages, chooses and buys; the ESC button leaves. In a headless check, by touch alone: three tiles right, two up, the counter faced, the box paged, BUY, and the next rod bought (0 to 1, 1000 to 910 coins).
207. **Debug and the gallery (40.2):** `dbg.shopWalk('RRRUU')` walks him a tile per letter through the walk's own rules (turns, collisions; the direction let go once each step has begun, else a held key strode on) and returns `{c, r, face, faced, blocked}`: from the mat to (9, 4) facing Rusty, then eight lefts to (2, 4) with one blocked by the cricket crate. The gallery's last page, `shop`, shows the baked room and every sprite (the fisher and the dog in each facing and frame, Rusty's frames and his back).
208. **Cleanup and cost (40.3):** the old painted room, leaning Rusty and tabbed panel went in Phase 37 (#193); now the last unused aliases (`hash`, `dither`). The room bakes in about 1 ms; a frame at night (the mask, the dust, every live thing) draws in 0.13 ms and updates in 0.002 ms in headless Chromium.

209. **The helm is the boat's, not the director's (41.3):** `fisher.helmStep` runs from `fisher.step` on `ctx.helm`, which `sailing.fisherStep` sets while the scene is `sailing` without passive and the boat is not stopped, the cord is being pulled (`b.startT ≥ 0`) or a direction is held with the motor running. While it runs the idle director is off (its context's mode is null) and any act ends at once (a can in hand is set on the gunwale). Stages: 1 the rod carried to the clips (fetched from the holder first if it rested there), 2 the near hand reaching back to the tiller, 3 steering; once the boat has stopped, 4 the hand back at the clips and 5 the rod carried up to the rod pose. Each waits for the hand (within `K.grab`, 0.8 px) and a least time (`HELM`: 0.3, 0.25, 0.25 s). A direction pressed again during 4 or 5 goes back to 2 or 1. Input is never held: the motor and the boat do exactly what they did before; the fisher only catches up.
210. **Where things are (41.1–41.2), local px:** the clips hold the rod's butt at (-3.2, -5.1) at 2° above the facing, so it lies along the near gunwale with its tip past the bow; two short posts (2 and 10 px along the rod) are drawn over the hull. The tiller runs from the motor head's front (-15, -5.4) to its grip at (-4.3, -6), swung up to 0.5 px forward and 0.3 px up by `steer` (the boat's acceleration × 0.01 plus 0.4 × throttle, eased at 6/s), with a rubber grip whose lit mark rolls with the throttle; held, the grip is the near hand (which leans with him). A first try at (-3.2, -6.2) and 4° read as a rifle across his chest, and a pivot at -12 floated in front of the motor; both moved after screenshots.
211. **The cord (41.2):** on a pull-start (`boat.M.start`, 0.4 s) the far hand reaches back to (-5.6, -5.4) for its first 35%, then pulls to (-1, -9.6), on the fast spring; a cream line runs from the motor's head to the far hand, drawn before him (the motor, drawn after him, covers its root). A motor still running (restarted within its 8 s rest) has no cord to pull.
212. **The lure under way:** the plan's keeper ring became the tip-top: while the rod is in the clips the hanging line is reeled to 1 px (`fs.line`), so the lure rides snug under the tip and barely swings, and it lets back out to 3 px as he takes the rod up; there is no jump of the lure. The clips' knock is `cue('clip')` (the knock buffer pitched up, quiet).
213. **Checks (41.4):** `dbg.sail(dir, s)` steers as a held key would (through `sailing.sailMove`), and `dbg.fisher()` gains `helm`, `helmT`, `clips`, `cord`, `steer`, `stows`, `tiller`. In headless Chromium (`?debug&seed=7` and `11`, every fisher step logged): a right run, a stop, a left run and a cast pressed 0.15 s after stopping: the hand on the tiller 0.46–0.57 s after the first move (≤ 1.0), the rod back in hand 0.45–0.85 s after the boat stops (≤ 1.2), the cast's charge started at once and the rod in hand 0.28 s later (≤ 0.4), launched from the hand; no hand moved more than 1.30 px in a step (≤ 2). `drawHelm` costs 0.016 ms. No page errors.
214. **The woof (42.1):** synthesised into the kit at unlock (`buildKit`, three takes, 0.24 s each): a pulse train falling from 330 Hz to about 215 Hz over 0.12 s through two resonators (550 and 1400 Hz, the second also fed a puff of noise at the start), a 6 ms rise, held 50 ms, then a 45 ms decay, its DC taken out by a one-pole high-pass. `cue('bark', x, pitch)` plays a take at gain 0.05 on the water bus (the water's reverb, muffled when the camera is under water), panned to the boat; `pitch` is the playback rate (1 for the golden dog; Phase 47 gives each dog its own). Its spectrum peaks at 540–565 Hz with no DC.
215. **When the dog speaks (42.2):** `dog.voice(d, dt, ctx, rng)` returns a woof to play (0 or 1) per step and is stepped by `sailing.update` (after `dog.step`) and by the shop. Idle bouts of 1–3 woofs every 180–480 s; reactions through `dog.request(d, why)` after a 0.4 s delay, 1–2 woofs: `jump` (the hooked fish leaving the water within 60 px of the boat), `catch` (a landing of any non-common fish or one of 2 kg or more), `bell` (entering the shop), `bird` (kept for Phase 49), `cue`. The plan's "at most one bout every 3–8 minutes" became: idle bouts 3–8 min apart, reactions only after 90 s without a bout, and no idle bout within 90 s of any other, so reactions still happen without the dog becoming noisy. Woofs are 0.35–0.55 s apart and the bark pose is held for the bout (not over a stare or a pet). Never while `ctx.block` (a fish biting, `run.bite.slot ≥ 0`, or the `hooked` state): a bout in progress stops and an asked-for one is dropped; an idle bout that falls due then, while the dog sleeps (passive) or is petted, waits 45 s. `G.vrng` only.
216. **The legendary cue is heard (42.2):** `cues()` now also asks for a `cue` bout (2 woofs), which skips the 90 s rule; with barks off it plays no woof and the silent `bark` pose shows as before (10.3).
217. **The setting (42.3):** `save.settings.dogBark` (default true). `save.migrate` now also fills settings added later from the defaults, so an old save gets `dogBark: true` without a version bump. The pause menu gains *Dog barks on/off* (Space or A/D) between Sound and Export; the board grew to 240 px tall for the sixth line. Off: no woofs at all, no idle or reaction bouts (an asked-for reaction is dropped).
218. **Checks (42):** `dbg.bark(why)` asks for a bout (no argument: the next idle bout in 1 s) and returns `{bouts, said, since, next, last, want, on}`. In headless Chromium, the voice run in the page for 30 simulated minutes three times (a 6 s bite window every 50 s, a reaction asked every 20 s): 18 bouts each (4–5 idle, 13–14 reactions), 29–30 woofs, **0 inside a bite window**, every two bouts at least 90.0 s apart, idle gaps 200–500 s; barks off: 0 bouts, 0 woofs; the legendary cue at Fogbound's `cold_hole`: off, the bark pose and 0 woofs; on, the pose and 2 woofs. Live: `dbg.bark('jump')` played one woof 0.4 s later (the engine's events `start, bark`); a bout's master peak −27.6 dBFS against −29.5 for a gunwale knock, the limiter at 0 dB of reduction. The pause menu toggles and the setting is saved; an old save without the field loads with it on. No page errors.
219. **Rods are a rack (43.1, 43.5):** `save.gear.rods` lists the rod indices owned and `save.gear.rod` is the one in hand; save v5's migration owns every rod up the ladder to the one in hand (each was bought on the way). The Ultralight Wand is appended as index 5, off the ladder (`ladder: false`), so older saves keep their indices. `economy.buy('rod')` (the pacing model's) buys the next rod up the ladder and takes it in hand; `buyRod(save, i)` buys any rod and `useRod(save, i)` takes an owned one in hand. Each rod has `power` (its name), `action` 0–4 (fed to `gfx/rod.js` in place of the tier index, so the bend is the rod's own), `smallHook`, `smallBoost` and `look` (its blank's two palette keys; boat.js and the shop's rack read it).
220. **Small fish and the rod (43.4):** `fight.start` multiplies the hook hold by the rod's `smallHook` when the fish's mass is under `rodSmall.frac` (0.15) × the rod's `maxMass`: Cane 1, Fibreglass 0.95, Graphite 0.9, Lacquered 0.82, The Long Rod 0.75, Wand 1. A light hold is thrown more easily in head shakes and jumps on a slack line (Phase 23). The wand's `smallBoost` 1.6 multiplies the species weight of fish of `rodSmall.mass` (0.4 kg) or less; it holds 1.2 kg and casts 104 px, for 60 coins. The plan's small-fish price bonus for the wand was left out: the boost and the hold are its reason to exist, and a price change would touch every sale.
221. **Lures (43.2):** five in `gear.lures` (Brass Spinner, Minnow Plug, Soft Grub, Cup Popper, Feather Jig) with `attract`, `rareBoost`, a price per lure and a pack size, and how they fish: `swim` ('mid', 'dive', 'bed', 'top', 'lift'), `sink` (× a bait's sink speed) and `maxDepthM`. The hook still holds one id in `save.gear.bait` and lures are counted in `save.baits` like baits (the plan's `gear.tackle` and `gear.lures` were not needed). `baitById` finds lures too; `isLure(id)` tells them apart.
222. **Lost and run out (43.5, the user's answer):** a lure is not used up by a cast; `economy.loseLure` takes one when the line **snaps** in active play (`underwater.catchStep`), the HUD says so for 4 s ("Lost the Brass Spinner. 1 left." or "None left: worms next.") and the save is written. A thrown hook does not take the lure (the hook came out of the fish, the lure is still on the line), and passive mode, live or away, never loses one: the boat fishing by itself should not empty the tackle while you are gone. With none left the hook falls back to worms, as baits always have.
223. **Who takes what (43.3):** each species has `lures: {id: 1 | 2}`: 2 loves it, 1 takes it, a lure not listed is ignored. Live baits still go by `baitLikes` (listed: interest 2; an empty list takes every bait), so worm, cricket and minnow play is unchanged. `fish_ai.interest(sp, bait, lure)` gives 0–2; the species weight is × 0.6, 1 or 2 by it (the baits' old `baitOther`/`baitLiked`), and a fish only notices the lure if its interest is over 0, from 1.3× its awareness for a lure it loves (`W.lureLoved`). An ignoring fish still turns up and swims past: in a 20-minute school run with each lure, none of the ignoring species noticed it once (e.g. Fogbound's spinner: gloam perch 124 spawned / 118 noticed, rust bream 36 / 0, mist carp 11 / 0). Every lure has takers in every water within the cane rod's mass and outside clock windows (`--validate` checks it). The journal's "Takes …" line adds "caught on …" for the lures a species has been landed on (`journal[id].took`, written by `recordCatch` from the card's `bait`).
224. **Where a lure fishes (43.6, the scene and the models):** in `underwater.lureStep` a lure sinks at `sink` × the bait speed down to `maxDepthM` (spinner 7 m, the grub and the jig to the bottom, the jig 1.8× as fast); the popper (`sink 0`, 'top') stays 0.5 px under the surface, is skated in along it, and a tap of Space pops it (a ring, a dent in the surface, its spit frame); the plug floats 2 px down, dives toward 4 m while reeled far from the boat and floats back up when let go. Fish rise to a surface lure: the species pick counts a 'top' lure as `gear.topDepthM` (1 m) deep. Passive mode and the pacing model fish a lure at its own depth (the plug at its diving depth, as a player reels it). In headless Chromium the five lures settled at: popper 0.13 m, plug 0.5 m (4.0 m after 1.2 s of reeling), spinner 7.0 m, grub 20.46 m and jig 20.95 m on a 20.5–21.25 m bottom.
225. **Lure art (43.6):** each lure is a shape in `underwater.BAITS` on the 12 px lure grid with its two phases: the spinner's blade face-on and edge-on (turning faster as it moves), the plug's flank flash on a tight wobble and its lip, the grub's curly tail, the popper's cupped face and spit (it lies level), the jig's lead head and its skirt flared while it moves. A first pass read as sticks at this size and was fattened. The gallery has a `lures` page; the shop paints an icon for each.
226. **The shop (43.5):** the rack lists all six rods (OWNED / ON, or a price; Space buys one or takes an owned one in hand) with the reel and line; the rack shows the rods not yet his in five slots in their blanks' colours. A lure case stands under the west window (tile 4, 1) and sells the lures in their packs (a description of how each swims, "Not every fish wants it. Lost if the line snaps."). Rusty's *Buy* lists 19 items, so the item window scrolls (9 rows, the choice kept in view). Until Phase 45's setup panel, the shop is where rods are switched.
227. **Balance (43.7):** `--tackle` fishes each hook for an hour at each water with that stage's rod (4 seeds) and counts net coins (baits per cast, lures when they snap). Net coins an hour (catches an hour), fog / creek / pines / red: worm 1071 (57.8) / 2751 (63.8) / 2386 (51.8) / 4761 (59.8); cricket 268 / 513 / 1952 / 1844; minnow 2134 / 6834 / 5674 / 10817; spinner 1723 / 2857 / 2469 / 4005; plug 1673 / 4450 / 1480 / 5099; grub 705 / 2142 / 1548 / 2540; popper 549 / 2166 / 2383 / 2456; jig 768 / 3934 / 2573 / 4356: every lure 50–200% of worms everywhere. The first run failed eight cells (the plug modelled floating, the popper fished at 0 m, too few creek takers); the fixes were the surface depth (#224), the plug fished at its diving depth, and more takers (popper: gloam perch, pier roach, blackwater chub; jig: mist carp, snag trout, blackwater chub, ash trout, porch catfish 2; plug: snag trout, ash trout, zigzag eel, lamp koi; grub: cabin dace, ash trout, porch catfish 2), the grub's attract 1.2 → 1.25. Prices per lure: spinner 18 (2 a pack), plug 26, grub 6 (4), popper 22, jig 14 (2).
228. **What did not move:** the pacing model fishes worms and buys rods up the ladder, and its rods' `smallHook` changes its holds only for small fish; `--replay 12345` is still `82118876` (no thrown hook in those 20 minutes changed), `--passive` 62.4% as before; `--fight` now uses each tier's rod hold (the table in 8.5).
229. **Small fish (44.1):** ten species appended after the 36 (so every index stays): Fogbound the Dim Fry, the Reed Stickleback and the Wick Minnow (at night); the creek the Gravel Darter and the Penny Dace; the Pines the Napkin Bluegill, the Jukebox Shiner (at night) and the Sugar Minnow (grey hours); the Red Room the Curtain Fry and the Thimble (uncommon, unease 0.3: the small strange one). 3–10 cm, 5–60 g, strength 0.2–0.3 and fatigue 0.25–0.3 (quick fights), each with its baits, its lures, a hint that is true of it and a description. `G.data.isSmall(f)` (`lengthCm[1]` ≤ `SMALL_CM` 10) keeps them out of V5's roster count; `--validate` wants two or three in each water within 3–10 cm and 5–60 g. All live at 0.5–5 m, so a shallow cast finds them and the usual 6–8 m rarely: a first pass with darters and minnows down to 8–9 m had the Pines model land 100+ sugar minnows a run and 6 of 20 seeds never open the Red Room.
230. **The size curve (44.2):** `economy.sizeQ(u, data)` = u^`economy.sizeCurve` (1.6) from the same single `rng.next()` as before, in `underwater.hook`, `passive.plan` and the pacing model; `economy.size(sp, q, out)` gives the length (0.1 cm) and weight (0.01 kg, or the gram under 0.1 kg). The card and the journal show a weight under 100 g in grams. `--validate` draws 10,000 per species: the median at most 0.339 of the range, the top 5% from 0.914 (the task wants under 0.5 and 0.9+). The mean size quantile fell from 0.5 to 0.385, so `sizeMin` went 0.7 → 0.85 to keep the mean `sizeFactor` at 1.1; without it the creek's median was 56.9 min.
231. **Small swimmers (44.3):** `fishgen.small(i)` sends a small species to `paintTiny`, a plain painter: a spindle body (back, flank and belly rows, deeper for perch, carp, koi and catfish), an eye, a dorsal pixel on the deep ones, a forked tail that flicks with the beat, narrowed through a turn, the belly flashing in a roll, the head nodding in a shake, a blanked column for a strange one's shimmer, and no outline. Its length is 1.5 view px a cm of the mean length (at least 6): 8–13 px. The portrait keeps the wrongness parts. The bare `paintLook` at that size left a hollow outline and was not used. The plan said `fishgen.swimmer` already scales by length; it does not (every species is drawn in its base's box), and the 36 older species were left as they are. The gallery has a `small` page (1× and 4×, the silhouette, the roll and the shake, the portrait).
232. **The rod feels the fish (44.3):** `rod.fishLoad(kg, maxMass)` = (kg / (0.15 × maxMass))^0.6, at least 0.08, 1 for anything heavier: it scales the fight's load, the hook-set's surge, the landing's weight and a head shake's kick on the tip (`sailing.js`). `--rod`'s 15th check: a 0.05 kg fish at load 0.6 moves the Ultralight Wand's tip 8.36 px and The Long Rod's 2.35 px (3.6×; a floor of 0.12 first gave 2.8×). In the browser a 14 g Dim Fry bent the wand −16° to −26° and the long rod −6° to −12°; before the kick was scaled the fry swung the cane's tip ±50°.
233. **Balance (44.4):** small fish's base prices 4–6 (the Thimble 6 × 2.5): at 2–4 the creek's median was 59 min. The Minnow Plug at the Pines fell to 36% of worms (`--tackle`, its takers there were the keyback bass and two deep fish), so the keyback bass loves it (`plug: 2`) and the diner perch takes it: 57%. Results in 8.5.
234. **What did not move:** the rods, `rodSmall`, the lures, their prices and losses, `passive.incomeMult` (0.63; passive 64.4% of active, in its band) and the fight constants. The replay's catch count went 18 → 19 in 20 minutes.
235. **The panel (46.1):** `G.ui.panel` draws, in the 640×360 view's pixels and palette keys only: `frame(ctx, x, y, w, h, title, aside, asideKey)`, a rectangle in `night_ink` with a one-pixel `paper` rim and 1 px cut corners, a title tab (bold fine font, `bone`) on the top edge at the left and an optional aside tab at the right (a price, the coins, NEW!), both opening into the body; `key` (a key cap, lit in `signal_amber` on `rust_dark` when chosen), `hint` (a cap and what it does, in `fog_mid`), `row` (a chosen row's bar), `place(w, h, anchor)` ('top', 'tl', 'tr', 'bl', 'br', 'right'; 8 px from the edges) and `inside`. A panel's bounds include its tab (13 px); content starts 6 px under it (`top`, `left`); rows are 12 px. `MAX_W` × `MAX_H` is 240 × 140. The aside was first drawn inside the body's top right; it ran into the first line of the tape and the shop's description, so it became a second tab.
236. **The catch card (46.2):** 232 × 82 at the top centre (204, 8), sliding down from above in 0.25 s; nothing is dimmed. The tab holds the name, the aside NEW! (`neon_red`) or RECORD (`teal_lit`); the body a portrait (80 × 40 box), "21.8 cm  0.35 kg", the rarity, the coin and the value, and the keys SELL Nc and KEEP with "A/D  SPACE". A/D and Space as before (Space sells by default, so a landing to a sale is still one key; the 0.4 s guard stays). A tap (`input.zone`) or a click (`mousedown`) on a key sets the choice through `card.tap` and presses the action. The species' line (`desc`) is no longer on the card: the journal page has always shown it. `card.rect()`, `keys()` and `choice()` are for checks.
237. **Pause (46.3):** a 176 × 136 list at the right edge (456, 112), titled PAUSED: Resume, Volume (ten ticks), Sound, Dog barks, Export save, Import save, a message from export or import wrapped to two lines, and the W/S and A/D hints. The keys are unchanged. The *Fisher & dog* line waits for Phase 47's picker (`TODO(P47.3)`). The world shows round it, still paused.
238. **The tape (46.3):** a 240 × 140 panel at the top centre: the tab "PLAY > AWAY" (or PASSIVE), the water's name on the aside tab, seven rows (was twelve over the whole screen), the SP counter and the totals, and the keys (SPACE all/close, W/S scroll when it scrolls). The VHS stays inside the frame: scanlines, a drifting tracking band, a row slipping a pixel, the whole frame slipping a pixel now and then. A row too wide for the panel (a long name) drops its length (`ROW_W`); everything else on it is as before.
239. **The map (46.3):** the chart stays as it was (a full-screen paper chart, not a small menu); its place strip became a 240 × 74 panel at the bottom left (8, 278), clear of every stop and label: the stop's name on the tab, its text wrapped to three lines, SPACE with what it does (`info.hint` is now only the verb: 'travel', 'go in', 'pay and travel') and M close.
240. **The shop's item window (46.3):** two 240-wide panels at the top right instead of the cream list, box and coins: the list (140 px, six 16 px rows with the item's icon at 14 px, the name, its price or OWNED/BEST, ON or the count; the shelf's name on the tab, BUY from Rusty; the coins with the coin on the aside tab; ^ and v at the foot when it scrolls), and the chosen item (98 px: the name on the tab, the price on the aside, four lines of description, the state or the last word, and SPACE/W/S/ESC). Colours moved to the dark panel's (`bone`, `rust_hi` prices, `moss_lit` ON and thanks, `rust_hi` for not enough coins). Rusty's text box and the Buy/Talk/Leave choice keep their Pokémon frame, and the journal its book, as the plan says.
241. **The `setup` action (45.1):** `G.core.input` gains `setup`. `KeyS` presses **both** `down` and `setup` (a key may now map to several actions, each held as its own source, `KeyS#0` and `KeyS#1`), so S still moves down every menu and walks the shop room, and `ArrowDown` presses `down` alone. On the boat, `down` pets the dog only when `setup` is not pressed on the same step: ↓ alone pets, S opens the panel (PLAN_TACKLE.md 2.5 and §4.1). The plan's "W/S move … S or Esc closes" asks S for two things in the panel; S closes it (it is the key that opened it), ↑/↓ and W move (wrapping), A/D or ←/→ switch column. `input.key(code, down)` drives a key by its code for tests.
242. **Petting by a tap (45.1):** `sailing.dogTap(gx, gy)` is true on the dog's sprite (12 × 10 world px, 2 px of slack round it), worked out from the boat's state the way `boat.draw` places him, only when he can be petted (the boat still between casts, not passive, no panel or overlay). A touch there presses `down` instead of the action, and so does a mouse click; elsewhere a click still casts.
243. **The panel (45.2):** `G.ui.setup`, a 240 × 132 panel at the top centre (200, 8), titled TACKLE: ROD and HOOK headers (the active one in `bone`), six rows a column. Rods: the owned ones in the rack's order, the name, power (six cells, `teal_lit`) and action (five cells, `signal_amber`) as two tiny bars, and the heaviest fish (`maxMass` kg). Hook: worms, then every bait and lure with any left (`x n`), then the one on the hook if it ran out (greyed in `fog_dark`, unpickable: "None left. Rusty sells more."). The Hook column scrolls (^ and v in its header) when more than six. What is in use is `signal_amber`; under the columns a line says what the cursor is on (power and action in words, or how a lure swims); SPACE pick, A/D column, S close.
