244. **Live while open (45.2):** not an overlay: the scene runs (the clock, drift, fish, the dog), but while it is open the panel has the keys: the boat does not sail, nothing is cast, the fisher's idle director waits (mode null), and Esc closes the panel instead of pausing. P, J, M and TAB still work (TAB closes it: passive has the line in the water). It opens only with `run.fsm === 'sailing'` and passive off, so with the line out S does nothing. A pick writes `save.gear.rod` (`economy.useRod`) or `save.gear.bait` at once and the scene recomputes `gearFx` after the panel's step, so the HUD, the next cast and passive use it from that step; closing autosaves ('setup') when anything changed.
245. **Touch (45.2):** an on-screen S button (528–564 × 332) next to M; TAB, J and M moved 32 px left to make room (the mouse shows the buttons from x 420). With the panel open, a tap on a row moves the cursor there and picks it; a tap elsewhere on the panel picks the cursor's row; a tap off the panel closes it.
246. **On the boat (45.3):** the drawn rod is `save.gear.rod` and the hanging lure `gearFx.bait`, as before, so both match the save in the frame after a pick. Closing with a change queues one of three new fisher acts (mode 'casts' only): `swap` (1.05 s: the chosen rod lies in the clips, his hand reaches and lifts it, a `clip` cue), `tie` (5.1 s, a short Phase 34 `lure` act: the rod upright in the holder, the lure let down to his face, baited or tied with the `bait` and `hooked` cues, a tug, reeled up), or `swaptie` (both). The act starts on the first quiet step with the boat stopped; any key ends it like every act, so a cast cuts it short. Under way the rod is in the clips and he takes it up when the boat stops (Phase 41), so a rod change there needs no `swap` (a hook change still plays `tie` once stopped).
247. **What did not move:** `js/sim` and `data` are untouched; the replay hash, pacing, passive, fight, rod and tackle numbers are unchanged. `dbg.setup()` reports the panel, the gear and the act queued or playing. The panel's sprites (it has none) need no gallery page; Phase 50.1's tackle page can show it.
248. **Look records (47.1):** `data/looks.js` (`G.data.looks`), loaded after `data/anomalies.js`. A fisher: `coat` (three keys: jacket and sleeves), `check` ('' or the key where a check shirt's bands cross), `hat` ('cap', 'knit', 'bucket', 'lamp') in `hatKeys` (three), `hair`, `beard` ('' or three), `skin` (four, dark to light), `trousers`. A dog: `coat`, `ear` and `muzzle` (three each), `chest`, `ears` ('drop', 'prick', 'heavy', 'pom'), `tail` ('feather', 'curl', 'low', 'pom'), `face` ('', 'mask', 'blaze'), `paleLegs`, `curly`, `bulk` (0.85–1.15, so every pose stays in its 24 × 20 box) and `bark` (the woof's playback rate, 0.6–1.5). The four fishers: Red cap (today's), Knit hat (an older man, a white beard, a teal knit hat with a bobble, a brown jacket), Rain hat (a yellow oilskin and bucket hat, darker skin), Headlamp (a red check shirt, a beanie with a headlamp, a dark beard). The dogs (PLAN_TACKLE.md §4.4): Golden (today's, bark ×1), Husky (grey, pale mask and legs, pricked ears, a tail curled over the back, ×1.15), St. Bernard (red-brown, a white blaze, chest, muzzle and legs, heavy ears, a low tail, bulk 1.12, ×0.78), Poodle (cream, curled coat, a topknot, fluffy ears, a pompom tail, bulk 0.92, ×1.3; a black poodle was tried and vanished against the night water). Names are placeholders for the user (2.8). `G.data.lookOf(list, id)` gives the record or the first for an unknown id. Ten palette keys added (`slicker_*`, `denim_*`, `umber_*`). `G.data.validateLooks(looks, palette)` (pure: the palette is passed in) checks four of each, unique ids, the shapes and that every key is in the palette; `--validate` runs it.
249. **The fisher's painter (47.2):** `fisher.js` paints the body, collar, three head tilts and both arms from the look's ramps (the coat with `ink` below it; the far arm a step darker, as before). Hats are a small table (`HATS`), each given the head's tilt for its brim. The shapes never change with the look (the user's §4.4: the same figure), so the shoulders `SH_N`/`SH_F` and the two bones are the same for every look and the IK elbow lands on the same pixel: checked by the arms' opaque masks, 168 arm images (every pose's near and far hand, both facings, three looks against Red cap), 0 differ. Paintings are cached per look (`arts[id]`); items and the cooler stay shared. `draw(..., lk)` and `armImg(..., lk)` take an optional look (the gallery and the picker); without it the look is `save.look.fisher`, read at draw time, so an import or a pick shows on the next frame with no wiring. `prebake(lk)` paints the body, collar, heads and the rod pose's arms both ways.
250. **The dog's painter (47.2):** `dog.js` sets the breed around each painting (`img`, cached per breed): `ear()` and `tail()` draw each shape on the existing poses (a husky's ear stands even when calm and does not stream in the wind; heavy and fluffy ears hang either side when it looks away), the muzzle takes its own ramp, `face` adds the mask or the blaze, `paleLegs` paints the front leg in the muzzle's keys, `curly` a dozen small rings, `bulk` scales the body's blobs and widens the chest patch. `prebake(lk)` paints every pose and frame both ways (22 images). `dog.barkPitch()` is the breed's `bark`; `sailing.js` and `shop.js` pass it to the bark cue instead of 1 (one line each, outside the task's file list, since 47.1's bark pitch has to reach the sound).
251. **The shop room's sprites (47.2):** `paintFisher` and `paintDog` take the look (trousers, coat, skin, the hat from above in four shapes, a beard facing down or to the side, the checks; the dog's ears, tail, pale legs, blaze or mask, curls and bulk). `sprite(kind, dir, fr, lk)` caches per look; Rusty has none. `shopRoom.prebake(kind, lk)` paints all sixteen facings and frames. The default look draws the same pixels as before in all three painters: hashes of every fisher pose × tilt × facing (`d67e31e7`), every dog pose × frame × facing with and without wind (`ea84fa05`) and every shop sprite (`335eef20`) equal the ones taken before the change.
252. **The picker (47.3):** `G.ui.look`, an overlay (the world waits behind it, like pause) in the small panel, 232 × 124, titled FISHER & DOG: two cells, FISHER and DOG, each a 56 px preview (him at 4×, hands on his knees, and the dog sitting, behind a gunwale and a hull) and the name between arrows; the chosen cell lit (`bone` heading, an amber edge, the name in `signal_amber`). W/S choose the cell, A/D turn through the looks (wrapping), Space or Esc is done. Pause → *Fisher & dog* (between Dog barks and Export save, with a `>`) opens it at the right edge; closing goes back to the pause list on that line. In the cabin A, or a tap on the door (the left edge, which presses `left` in a menu scene), opens it at the bottom left; closing goes back to the room. The pause list grew to 176 × 140 for its seventh line (the message from 103 px). `menu.open(name, arg)` passes `arg` to the overlay's `open`.
253. **`save.look` (47.3):** `{ fisher, dog }` ids, default `{ fisher: 'red_cap', dog: 'golden' }`. A turn writes it at once and bakes the new look (`fisher.prebake` or `dog.prebake`, and `shopRoom.prebake`), 2.6–5.4 ms per look on first use in headless Chromium (the budget is 60 ms); closing autosaves ('look') when anything changed. No version bump: `migrate()` fills a missing `look` like any later field, and a look that is not an object, or an id that is not a string, goes back to the default; an unknown string id is drawn as the default (`lookOf`). `--saves` gains a v5 save from Phase 46 (no look), one with a look (kept) and one with `look: { fisher: 7 }` (reset), and checks the look of each.
254. **Gallery and what did not move:** three gallery pages: `fishers` (every look in every pose, its head and item as the acts have them, both facings, at 1×), `dogs` (every breed in every pose and frame at 2×, the wind on the alert ears) and `looks top` (the shop room's sprites for every look). They are here, not in 50.1, because 47.2's check is the gallery; 50.1's `dbg.look(f, d)` waits for Phase 50 (`G.ui.look.set(kind, id)` does the same now). `js/sim` is untouched and `data/looks.js` is read only by the painters, the picker and the checks: the replay hash, pacing, passive, fight, rod and tackle numbers are unchanged.

255. **Where the life lives (48.1–48.3):** a new `js/scene/life.js` (loaded after `underwater.js`), presentation only: `G.vrng`, typed arrays sized once, stepped from `underwater.viewStep` on every step (also while sailing, so the bed has gone on living when the view goes under; 0.02 ms a step on average) and drawn from `underwater.draw` in two passes: `drawBed` after the bed and the caustics (crayfish, snails, the eel, silt) and `drawWater` after the weeds (fry, tadpoles, the darting perch, the turtle), both before the school. Coordinates are world x and depth below the waterline, as the school's. One table per water, `life.WATERS` (fog, creek, pines, red): how many crayfish, snails, fry clouds and tadpole clouds, whether a turtle and an eel live there, and what the bake paints (mussels, caddis cases, litter, roots, a drowned tree, algae, ribbon, fern and lily). The table is in the scene file, not `data/loc_*.js`: 48's tasks list only scene files, and 49.2 is the task that adds `life` tables to the location data, so it may move there then.
256. **Bed life (48.1):** crayfish (5 view px tall, two leg frames) walk at 2 px/s, rest 2–6 s, turn; a splash within 36 + 30 × power px across makes each back off after 0.08 + d/400 s (at most 0.28 s; measured 0.083–0.200 s for 0–42 px), facing the splash and moving tail first at 16 px/s easing to 0 over 0.7 s with a puff of silt; the lure or anything else within 12 px does too. Snails crawl over the boulders (0.25 px/s, turning at their edges) and pull into their shells for 4 s at a splash within 30 px. To react to splashes `fx.listen` now keeps a list of listeners instead of one (the far shoals' startle still fires: 6 startled after a splash in the check). Mussels (half buried: a shape is cut at the bed's top), caddis cases on the creek's snags, litter (bottles, a lost lure with its line on a snag or the bed, tyres in the Pines), old roots and a drowned tree with its branches are painted into the bed layer; the bake now records the boulders (`set.rocks`), snags (`set.snagAt`) and weed strands (`set.strands`) for the snails and the fry.
257. **Fry, tadpoles, visitors (48.2):** clouds of 14 fry by the weed strands (spread along the water, at 0.5–0.9 of a strand's height) and of 6 tadpoles near the two shores 5–13 px under the surface (there are no shallows in the cross-section: every bed is at least maxDepth − bottomAmp deep, so "in the shallows" became "near the shore and the surface"). Each member springs to its place in a loose grid (K 2.2, D 2.6) with a slow wobble and is pushed away (420 px/s² × closeness) from every threat within its reach: school fish 18 px, the hooked fish 24, the lure 14, the perch 26, the turtle 26, the eel 20; then a hard spacing pass (three passes, as the far shoals') keeps any two at least 2 px apart across or 1 px apart down, so their 1.5 × 0.5 px bodies never overlap. A perch silhouette darts through a cloud in view every 35–80 s; a turtle crosses the view at mid depth every 70–150 s where the table has one (Fogbound, the Pines); an eel slides along the bed at night every 50–110 s, its 14-segment body following its head, puffing silt. Fry are drawn as two fills (a `water_foam` body, an `ink` eye) at 0.8 alpha, tadpoles, crayfish, snails and the turtle as small baked sprites of view-pixel rows in palette keys, the perch as the shoals' perch silhouette. Clouds more than 400 px from the view are not stepped.
258. **Silt (48.1, 48.3):** a pool of 64 motes in `S.shift(silt, 1)`, puffed up by a walking crayfish (one every 0.8 s), a backing one (5), the eel (one every 0.5 s), the lure on the bed moving (2 every 0.2 s: dragged, drifting or hopped), and a school fish within 4 px of the bed swimming faster than 1.5 px/s (one every 0.6 s); they rise 1.5–4 px/s, slow, drift with the current and fade over 1.2–2.4 s.
259. **More plants (48.3):** in the three weed frames, from their own stream (seed × 31 + 204; the bed's things use + 203, so the older layout is unchanged): ribbon weed (clumps of 2–4 blades 16–46 px tall, one pixel wide and two where they twist), a fern-like weed (a stem with paired leaflets shortening to the tip) and lily stems from the bed to a pad lying on the surface, near the shores; algae coat the tops of a share of the boulders. All in the water's weed keys, so the Red Room's are crimson. Each water's mix: Fogbound ribbon 0.025/px, fern 0.02, 5 lilies, algae on 60% of boulders; the creek ribbon 0.03, fern 0.008, no lilies, 50%; the Pines 0.012, 0.02, 3 lilies, 30%; the Red Room 0.012, 0.01, none, 20%. The cross-section's bake per water measured 65–165 ms before and after (noise; it includes the roster's swimmers).
260. **Left for Phase 49:** the diving duck's feet and body and the heron's beak through the surface (2.9's food-chain beat) need the birds above, so they are a `TODO(P49.1)` in `life.js`. The plan's "a predator fish darts at the fry" is the perch silhouette, not a school fish: the school is the simulation's and is never moved by presentation.
261. **dbg and the overlay:** `dbg.life()` (crayfish with mode and pending reaction, snails, clouds with `off` (the furthest member from its place), `calm` (s since anything came near) and `overlaps`, the visitors, silt, the last splash's slowest reaction, the visitors' timers, step and draw ms) and `dbg.lifeSpawn('turtle' | 'eel' | 'dart')`, refused where the water's table has none; an overlay line `life cray … snails … clouds … silt …`. The gallery page for life is 50.1's.
262. **Where the life above lives (49.1–49.4):** the second half of `js/scene/life.js`, presentation only (`G.vrng`, a few small arrays, stepped first thing in `life.step`, every step, also while sailing; at dusk with six creatures out the step is 0.11 ms). The tables are in the location files now (`loc.life`): `under` is 48's table moved verbatim (the bakes are unchanged) and `above` names each creature with its `hours` (`[[from, to], ...]`, wrapping past midnight), where it lives (`x` ranges in its own layer's px with its parallax `p`, `perches` as {x, y above the waterline}, or the painted `layer` it walks on) and the loon's `call` hours; `--validate` checks them (`validateLife`: known creatures, hours in 0..24, places inside the world or the layer, a lodge for the beaver, lilies for the frog, perches for the kingfisher and the gulls, known shore features). Four draw passes: `drawFar` just before the landmarks layer (the ducks and the loon, further out than the boat at p 0.9 and 0.85, so the boat passes in front of them), `afterLayer` after every layer (the deer and the beaver on the bank, the fox on the far shore, a moored boat's lantern, and after the landmarks the heron, the kingfisher, the gulls, the dragonflies), `drawSurface` after the waterline (the lily pads, the frog, the striders) and `drawUnder` from 48's `drawWater` (the heron's beak, neck and legs below the surface, the hen diving, the loon under, the kingfisher's moment under, the frog swimming down). The backdrop is also what the mirror pass draws, so the birds, the deer and the lantern reflect; `life.mirror(on)` tells the deer when it is drawn there. What a bird has under water is a threat to the fry and tadpoles too (they part around it).
263. **Water birds (49.1):** *Ducks* (Fogbound, the creek, the Pines): a hen (mottled brown, an eye stripe, a teal wing patch; a hen, not a drake, leads young) and 3–4 young following in a line, paddling at 2 px/s to points in their stretch. Within 64 screen px of the boat they paddle away at 7 px/s; at the end of their stretch they slip past behind the boat (they are further out) until it is 64 px behind, and if it follows them round they turn away again. Every 25–50 s, with the boat over 128 px off, the hen tips up (0.4 s), goes under with a ring and a dent, swims down to 16 px and along at 3–6 px/s with her feet paddling (drawn in the cross-section) and comes up 3.5 s later; she leaves at once if she surfaces near the boat. *Heron* (all three): stands in the shallows of a stretch near a shore (p 1), turns, and stalks at 1.5 px/s toward the nearest fry or tadpole within 12 px of the surface in its stretch; otherwise, one time in three, it strikes at something we cannot see. The strike lasts 0.75 s (the neck's reach eases out in 0.18 s, holds 0.15, comes back in 0.42): at 0.18 s a splash (`fx.splash` 0.1, so 48's crayfish and snails and the far shoals hear it), a ring, the cue `strike`, and it takes a fry or tadpole within 8 px of its beak (gone 20–40 s, then back in its place in the cloud) and holds it 2.5 s. The neck is a curve from the shoulder to the head (rest, stalk and strike shapes), drawn above the waterline in the scene and below it in the cross-section with the legs. With the boat within 45 px, or its hours over, it flies off slowly away from the boat and fades, back 40–80 s later. *Kingfisher* (all three): on perches from the data (Fogbound's pier lamp and two markers, the creek's cabin roofs, the Pines' owl branch and the signal's arm); every 6–14 s, with the boat over 80 px off, it drops to the water in 0.35 s, is under 0.3 s, takes a fry within 8 px if there is one, and flies back up in 0.6 s. With the boat within 40 px (also as it lands back) it flies at 34 px/s to the perch farthest from the boat at least 80 px away, or leaves. *Loon* (Fogbound, the Pines, p 0.85): swims at 1.5 px/s and dives every 20–45 s, or at once when the boat comes within 55 screen px; under 5–11 s at 12 px/s down to 14–24 px (in the cross-section), coming up only once it is at least 82 px from the boat. In its call hours (dawn and dusk) it calls every 40–90 s, never at the liminal weight. *Audio* (`engine.cue`): `loon`, three sine glides (520→770 Hz, 770→790 held 1.4 s, 790→590) with 0.2–0.25 s attacks, gain 0.024 × (0.4 + 0.6 × nearness) on the water bus (its reverb, muffled under water), the far shore giving it back at 0.3 after 2.2 s; `strike`, `dive`, `plop` and `slap` are splashes of size 0.12, 0.08, 0.05 and 0.2 (a lure landing is 0.16–0.21). In the 8-minute sweeps below the creek's and the Pines' herons struck 23 and 17 times and took nothing (no fry come near the surface there); Fogbound's struck 11 times and took 11.
264. **Small life and the shore (49.2):** *Dragonflies* in two zones near the shores by day, darting at 40 px/s between hovers of 0.4–2 s, 3–14 px above the water, never within 24 px of the boat. *The frog* lives on the lily pads (Fogbound 5, the Pines 3: the cross-section's lilies, `set.lilies`, now also drawn lying on the surface from above); its throat pulses; within 50 px of the boat it jumps 6 px away, plops in (a ring, `plop`) and swims down and away for 1.6 s (in the cross-section), back 25–50 s later on the pad farthest from the boat. *Striders* skate in strokes of 8–14 px/s, each stroke a 2.5 px ring. *The deer* (Fogbound's shore, the creek's bank, at their parallax) at dawn and dusk walks in, drinks with its head down, and looks up every 4–9 s; within 70 screen px of the boat it looks up for 1.2 s, then walks off away from it fading over 2.5 s, not back for 60–120 s. *The beaver* (Fogbound, the creek, dusk to dawn) starts 70–140 px from its lodge (a feature of its layer) and swims to it with a V behind its head, diving there; within 50 screen px of the boat it slaps its tail (`slap`) and dives, away 60–120 s. *The fox* (the Pines at night) trots across the diner's lot at 6 px/s, sits under the lamps up to twice for 3–6 s, and fades at either end or when its hours end. *Gulls* (the Pines by day) stand on the pier's rail and lamp; within 45 px of the boat they lift off and circle 40–52 px out from their perch on the far side of it, and come back after 8 s once it is 80 px off. The hours are each water's table (`life.above`); the Red Room's is empty: indoors, nothing lives above the water.
265. **Background objects (49.3):** `paint.js` has three more silhouettes (a spruce spire, a dead tree with a broken top, a poplar column; a kind without its own keys borrows pine's or round's), now in the mixes of Fogbound's two forests, the creek's and both of the Pines'. `shore()` paints a layer's `features` behind its rocks (`posts`: an old dock's n posts, weathered to different heights, a stringer across two; `moored`: a rowboat on the water with its seat, an oar, the painter to a stake and a lantern on a pole at the stern; `lodge`: a dome of sticks and mud; `duckbox`: a nesting box on a pole in the water), and after everything it painted before, from the same stream, `trunks` fallen trunks (a root plate on the bank, the trunk sloping into the water, broken limbs), `cattails` clumps (stalks, brown heads, curving blades) and `pads` lily pads on the water (some with a flower). `forest()` and `shore()` return the kinds they painted, which sailing keeps per layer (`dbg.shore()`, with each layer's first bake time). A layer's `place` seed, when it has one, replaces `loc.seed × 31 + i` for its stream, so a layer can be re-rolled alone; the three banks and two forests have one. The Red Room's floor gets things standing on it at the water's edge (`scenery.js`, `ROOM_THINGS`): a potted fern, a straight chair, a side table with an unlit lamp and a door frame opening on nothing. The moored boats' lanterns are lit at night from `life.afterLayer` (a flickering point and a faint pool), so they reflect. Every shore and floor uses 5–10 kinds of thing (below). The layers' bakes did not move: a first load measured 130–142 ms for the creek before and 135–155 after, the Pines 124–136 and 124–126, the Red Room 59–119 and 55–67, the boot's bakes 263–289 and 270–312 ms (three runs each, noise), all behind the map's fade as before.
266. **The liminal touches (49.4):** from unease 0.6 (`LA.limU`) a weight comes in at 0.25/s (0 to 1 in 4 s) and goes the same way. At over half of it: the ducks stop (they slow at 0.8 px/s²) and every 1.2 s one more of them turns to face the boat, the hen first, and they keep facing it as it moves; they do not dive. At night the heron stands all night: it comes even outside its hours (fading in over 4 s at the middle of its stretch), finishes a strike under way, and then stands with its neck up, never striking, turning or flying, even with the boat beside it. The deer's reflection fades over 3 s (the mirror pass draws it at 1 − the weight), the deer itself does not change. The loon stops calling. Nothing in the touches makes a sound, and nothing moves faster than it did.
267. **What else changed:** `life.WATERS` is gone (`life.table(id)` reads `loc.life.under`); `underwater.prepare` records the lilies (`set.lilies`, world x and pad width, from the plants of 48.3); 48's `TODO(P49.1)` is done (the diving hen and the heron's strike under the surface). A fry or tadpole a bird took is hidden for 20–40 s and comes back at its place (the clouds keep their size). The gallery's life page is 50.1's, as the plan has it.
268. **dbg and the overlay:** `dbg.lifeAbove()` (each creature of the water's table: alpha, place, px from the boat on screen, what it is doing; the touches and the liminal weight; how many strikes, takes, dives, plops, calls and slaps), `dbg.lifeShowing()` (who is seen now), `dbg.lifeSpawn` with `'ducks'`, `'heron'`, `'kingfisher'`, `'loon'`, `'frog'`, `'deer'`, `'beaver'`, `'fox'` (out now, in their hours) and `'strike'`, `'duckdive'`, `'loondive'`, `'kingdive'`, `'call'`, and `dbg.shore()` (each layer's kinds and bake ms); an overlay line `above … heron … ducks … lim …` with FACE, STILL and NO-REFLECTION when the touches are on.
269. **Gallery pages (50.1):** six pages after `looks top`. `tackle`: each rod's blank drawn straight at 2× in its own keys (cork, seat, the look's two keys, the guides) with the setup panel's power and action bars, its power, action, maxMass, castMax, smallHook (and the wand's small-fish boost) and price; every hook (four baits, five lures) as its body at rest and moving ×3, with its kind, swim, price and pack and how many species take it. `setup`: the real TACKLE panel (`G.ui.setup`, opened from the gallery's update when the page comes up and closed when it goes) over sky and water, from the save. `life under` and `life above`: every sprite in `life.js` (14: cray, snail, turtle, tad; duck, duckling, duckUnder, kingfisher, loon, frog, deer, fox, gull, heron) with all its frames at 1×, then ×4 (×3 above), then flipped; then what is drawn in code, at 2× the game: the eel both ways, a fry cloud and the darting perch; the heron standing, stalking, striking (reach 0.5 and 1, its beak and neck below the surface as the cross-section shows them) and flying, the beaver swimming and diving, a dragonfly, a strider and the moored boat's lantern. `bed`: each water's bed things and new weeds on a strip 320 world px wide (`underwater.galleryBed(id)`: a flat bed, two boulders for the algae, one snag for the caddis and the lost lure, then `bedThings` and the plants from the water's own table and keys), labelled with its table. `shore`: the four shore things (`paint.SHORE_THINGS`), a trunk, cattails and pads (`paint.trunk`, `cattails`, `pad`, now exported), every tree kind in the forest keys of Fogbound, the creek and the Pines, and the Red Room's floor things (`scenery.ROOM_THINGS`, now exported) on its zigzag; baked once (`gallery:shore`). `gallery.page()` names the page shown, for the checks.
270. **Posing life for the gallery:** the code-drawn creatures read their live record, so the painters take one: `drawHeron(c, camPx, wyS, under, H)` and `heronNeck(camPx, wyS, H)` (H defaults to the heron), `drawBeaver(c, X, wyS, B)`, and the eel, the dragonfly and the strider came out of their loops as `drawEel(ctx, X, Y, dir, camPx, wyS)`, `dragonfly(c, X, Y, fl, a)` and `strider(c, X, y)`. `life.gallery` poses them from its own scratch records (`gH`, `gB`, `gEx`/`gEy`), never the live ones; `life.sprites()` bakes and returns every sprite. The game draws the same pixels as before: 13 frames of four scenes (dusk with ducks, the loon, the frog, the deer and the beaver; noon with the kingfisher, dragonflies and striders; night under water; night with the heron and the eel), stepped one frame a step from a manual `requestAnimationFrame`, hash the same on the Phase 49 code and this one.
271. **`dbg.tackle(rod, hook)` and `dbg.look(f, d)` (50.1):** `dbg.tackle(rod, hook)` puts an owned rod (index or id) in hand and a hook you have on (a bait or lure id), as the setup panel would: `gearFx` is worked out at once, the save is written ('setup') and the fisher's act is queued through `sailing.queueTackle(rod, hook)` (the panel's own path, `tackleAfter`, now calls it too); null or nothing leaves one as it is, refused gear is listed in `refused`, and the result is the old listing, now also `dbg.tackleNow()`. `dbg.look(f, d)` sets the looks by id through `G.ui.look.set` (baked at once), saves ('look') when anything changed, and returns the look drawn and any refused ids; `dbg.look()` lists the ids. `dbg.life()` was already there (Phase 48).
272. **The night frame (50.2):** headless Chromium at 1280×720, Fogbound at 21:30 (drizzle 0.78 at unease 1), the liminal heron standing still, the loon, the frog and the beaver out, an anomaly on in 720–721 of 722 frames (forced again when one ended), 12 s each: above the water with `reflected_figure`, 60 fps every second, the frame interval median 16.7 ms (p95 17.2, worst 23.3), update 0.25 ms, draw 3.35 ms, the worst frame's cost 3.6–5.0 ms a second, life's step and draws 0.1 ms (median), the reflection pass 2.7 ms median (p95 3.3); with `empty_boat`, draw 3.12 ms, worst 4.0–6.4 ms; under water (8 fish, the eel, the turtle, 7 crayfish, 8 snails, 7 clouds, drizzle above), draw 0.71 ms, update 0.49 ms, worst 1.6–2.8 ms. The same surface scene on the code before Phase 48: draw 2.95 ms and the reflection 2.7 ms, so the life adds about 0.2–0.4 ms of drawing and nothing to the reflection, whose 2 ms budget (3.4) this machine missed before (8.4 #26). Nothing needed optimising.
273. **What did not move:** `js/sim` and `data` are untouched; the replay hash, pacing, passive, fight, saves, rod and tackle numbers are as in 8.5.
274. **RELEASE on the card (51.1):** the card has three keys, SELL, KEEP and RELEASE (A/D move, Space or a tap picks; `G.ui.card.keys().release`). `economy.recordCatch(save, c, 'release')` writes the journal as any catch (count, best, hints, the window flag, the bait) and adds `j.released`; no coins and nothing on the plaques. The journal's count line says "Caught 3 times, 1 let go". No new state in the FSM: the card goes back to `sailing` as before, and the release is presentation only.
275. **The toss (51.2):** `sailing.js` `releaseStart/releaseStep/drawRelease` and `fisher.toss` (s into the swing, -1 otherwise). The far hand holds the fish low by the gunwale (0–0.12 s), swings it back past his hip (to 0.42 s), out and up over the water (to 0.58 s, a fast spring) and back to his knee; the fish leaves his hand at 0.52 s with (58 px/s along the facing, −62 px/s up) under 240 px/s², drawn as its portrait sized by its length (8–34 view px). Where it meets the surface (`floatY`): a splash, a ring and a dent sized by its weight, and the 'jump' back-in sound; then its swimmer slips away 34 px out and 9 px down over 1.6 s, fading. A cast, the helm or any state change lets it go at once (a short drop beside the boat). No draws from `G.rng`: the replay hash is unchanged.
276. **Checks (Phase 51):** headless Chromium, `?debug&seed=7`, Fogbound at 12:00: `dbg.give('gloam_perch', true)`, → → Space on the card: `choice` 'release', the far hand at (0.21, −6.13) at 0.32 s, (6.39, −10.07) at 0.52 s with the fish flown, in the water at 1.0 s 35 px out; coins 0 → 0, `kept` 0, the journal entry `released: 1`; no page errors. `dbg.release()` reads it. `--validate`, `--replay 12345` (1966984761), `--saves` as in 8.5.
277. **What went (52.1):** the TAB action, its key and its on-screen button (`input.js`); `sailing.js` passiveOn/Off/Step, the float, the passive rod pose, the passive lift (`plift`), `setPassive` and the overlay line; the HUD's "TAB passive" and its notes; the audio engine's float and coin cues; `underwater.js` hooking, fighting (the auto-player at skill 0.8) and keeping lures for passive; the time-away call at boot and `?compact`'s passive start (`?compact` is now only the small view); `dbg.passive` (a stub that says so), `dbg.away` and `dbg.tape`. `G.ui.tape` and `G.sim.passive` no longer exist. `bite.hold(t, data)` lost its `auto` argument and `fight.holdPassive` its constant. `fight.autoPlayer` stays: the balance tool's player model uses it.
278. **What stayed (52.2):** the save keeps `lastSeen` and `tapes` (old saves carry them; nothing writes tapes now), and journal pages marked `phantom` by an old tape still read as before. The idle director, the dog and the setup panel lost their "not passive" conditions and nothing else. This retires V7 and decision D5 (passive at 60% of active), at the user's request; §0–§7 and Appendix V are left as written.
279. **Checks (Phase 52):** `--passive` is three PASS lines: no passive sim and no `passive.incomeMult`; no TAB key, button or time-away call at boot; a save away for hours loads with the coins and journal it was saved with. `--validate` errors if `gear.passive` or `G.sim.passive` come back. Headless Chromium: TAB does nothing (`run.passive` undefined), a catch sells for 10c, the journal, map, pause and the shop open and close, no page errors. The replay hash, fight and saves are unchanged (the active model never used passive).
280. **The data (53.1):** `gear.lineTypes`: monofilament (free; every factor 1, so every number tuned before Phase 53 is its), fluorocarbon (140c: strength ×0.95, tension rise ×1.06, slack-shake hold loss ×1.08, +0.03 hook-set hold, unseen, feel ×1.25) and braided (240c: strength ×1.35, rise ×1.2, shake ×1.4, +0.08 hold, seen, feel ×1.8). The five line tiers are now the line's test, 6/8/12/17/25 lb (ids, costs and strengths unchanged; "Braided Line" was a tier name and is now a type). `economy.effects` gives `lineStrength` (test × type), `lineType`, `lineRise`, `lineShake`, `lineHold`, `lineSeen`, `lineFeel`; `buyLineType`, `useLineType`, `ownsLineType`, `lineTypeById`. Save v6 (`gear.lineType`, `gear.lineTypes`; the v5 → v6 migration gives every older save monofilament).
281. **The fight (53.2):** `fight.start` reads `lineRise`, `lineShake`, `lineHold`: the pulling fish's tension rise and a jump's spike are × rise, the hold lost in a slack shake or jump × shake, and the hook-set's hold + hold (at most 1). So braid (no stretch) takes every lunge harder but is so much stronger that it snaps less, sets the hook firmly, and throws more hooks when the line goes slack in a shake; fluorocarbon is a little less forgiving than mono. `--fight` on tier 2 at skill 0.5: mono lands 78.5% (6.3% snapped, 15.2% thrown), fluorocarbon 75.5% (9.6%, 14.9%), braid 71.0% (3.7%, 25.4%); at 0.85 all 96.6–98.0%.
282. **Line-shy fish (53.3):** `fish.lineShy` on the ash trout (creek, rare), the neon koi (Pines, uncommon) and the lamp koi (Red Room, uncommon). In `fish_ai` such a fish, having circled the lure, turns away instead of nibbling when `ctx.lineSeen` (the line type's `seen`), and is marked `shied`. The scene says "It saw the line and turned away." once a cast and writes the journal hint 'line' for its species ("Shy of the line: it saw mine and turned away. Only fluorocarbon fools it."), caught or not. A first try also made the mist carp (Fogbound) line-shy: with no fluorocarbon early that cost the pacing model 11 minutes to the creek, so Fogbound has none. The Red Room's key fish may never be line-shy (`--validate`).
283. **Feel and look (53.4):** a nibble's and a bite's nods of the rod tip × `lineFeel` (braid shows every touch); the line drawn in the type's key and alpha (mono fog_light 0.6, fluorocarbon fog_light 0.28, braid moss_hi 0.8; brighter while fighting), along the rod too; under water it sinks at the type's `sinkG` (`rope.setSink`: braid 1.5 px/s², mono 3, fluorocarbon 6), and a waiting line keeps the type's belly (mono 1.03, fluorocarbon 1.025, braid 1.012). The shop's rack has the three spools (buy, or spool on one you own) with Rusty's words for each; the setup panel has a third column, LINE (it is up to 320 px wide now, past the small panel's 240), and a pick needs no act from the fisher. `dbg.line(id)`.
284. **The model (53):** `tools/balance.js` buys fluorocarbon after the creek (PLAN gains 'fluoro'); `--tackle` compares lures with the line left out (`unseen`: line-shy fish bite, the fight is mono's), which reproduces its pre-53 table exactly. `--validate` checks the line types (mono first and all ones, at least one unseen, keys, `lineShy` true or absent) and prints the line-shy species; `--saves` gains a v6 save with braid on the reel.
285. **Checks (Phase 53):** `--validate` (and `--strict`) OK; `--replay 12345` still 1966984761 (20 minutes never reach a line-shy fish); `--pacing` all PASS, medians 6.0 catches, creek 45.1 min, Red Room 345.6 min; `--fight` seven PASS lines; `--tackle` PASS (as before); `--saves` 11 PASS lines; `--rod` unchanged. Headless Chromium: on mono a line-shy fish set to finish its circle turns away (`shied`, the journal hint written); on fluorocarbon it nibbles; the setup panel lists the three lines; no page errors.
286. **The journal (54.1):** two panels side by side (`LP` 8,8 300×344 and `RP` 316,8 316×344) replace Phase 17's open book (its bake, `journal:book640`, is gone). Left: the fish's name on the tab and NEW or its rarity beside it in a rarity key (common fog_light, uncommon teal_lit, rare signal_amber, legendary neon_red); the portrait on a night_deep plate (80 px); where it lives with a pin, the count, "Best …" in amber; a rule; then the notes, still the handwriting that degrades with unease, up to 14 lines, the book's ink keys mapped for night ink (`INK`: ink → bone, wood → fog_mid, rust → dusk_high, the late blue pen → teal_lit). Right: JOURNAL on the tab with "caught / shown" beside it, the two filters as key caps (lit when focused), the 5×5 grid of 52×46 tiles (night_deep, fog rims, the chosen one rust_dark with an amber rim, NEW as a neon_red square, a late line as a teal `*`), a thin scrollbar, the heavy-fish margin note, and the keys. The controls and the logic are unchanged.
287. **The map (54.2):** the chart is a panel, THE WATERS on its tab and the coins beside it, over the world (no dim; the travel fades stay). The chart (`map:chart640night`, 608×322) is repainted in the panel's colours: night_ink with a faint dotted grid for the folds, peaks in fog_dark and night_blue, pines in moss_dark, the route dotted in fog_mid, lakes in water_mid inside a water_hi band and a fog_light shore (the Red Room in crimson, blood and dusk_rose), the compass in fog with an amber needle. Stops are bone squares (fog_mid when shut); names bone, fog_mid when shut, and the chosen one amber on the list's bar. The place card sits inside the panel (10 px in from its corner).
288. **The shop and the cabin (54.3):** Rusty's text box is a panel with RUSTY on its tab, his words in bone (a reversed line in neon_red), the more-to-read arrow in amber; Buy/Talk/Leave is a small panel list with the chosen row on the list's bar. The Pokémon-style cream window (`frame`) is gone. The cabin's title, next restoration, icebox and keys are a 300×50 panel at the top centre ("THE CABIN: DERELICT" on the tab, the coins beside it, the keys as caps).
289. **Checks (Phase 54):** headless Chromium (`?debug&seed=7`): the journal with 12 species recorded, the map, Rusty's box (walked with `dbg.shopWalk('UUURR')`), the item window and the cabin, each screenshotted and looked at; no page errors. Every menu now draws through `G.ui.panel.frame`: `grep -n "M.dim" js/ui` finds only the map's travel fades. Presentation only: no `js/sim` or `data` change.
290. **When and how strong (55.1):** `stormK()` in `sailing.js`: an hour is stormy when `hash(loc.seed, hour × 31 + 7) % 11 === 0` (Fogbound 3 hours of 24, the creek 3, the Pines 1, never the Red Room, whose sky has no clouds), rising over the hour's first fifth and falling over its last. `rainK()` takes the larger of the drizzle and the storm, so everything the drizzle does (rings, the softened mirror, the shop's rain on the glass and its sound) comes with it; the rain is up to 2.4× as dense, falls faster and slants more; the surface's energy gains 0.8 × k; night_ink at 0.32 × k darkens the sky; the night and grey clouds thicken by k and the stars and moon fade by k (the moon's light on the water with it).
291. **Lightning (55.2):** every 6–18 s divided by k (G.vrng) a flash: fog_light over the sky at up to 0.4 and over the water at up to 0.18, a flicker (0.7 for 0.06 s, 0.2, then 1 for 0.08 s, then fading at e^−6t), dimmer the further off (×1 − dist/2); half the time a bolt, nine jagged segments from the clouds down behind the far ridge with a three-segment branch, drawn before the hills so they stand in front of it, for the flash's first 0.35 s. It is drawn in `drawBackdrop`, so the mirror shows it too. fog_light is darker than dusk_high, so the flash lightens the sky toward a colour under the ceiling (§0.9); measured over 30 frames with three flashes against 30 without, 6 pixels (of ~140,000) came out above the ceiling, all where a bright pixel had moved (the pier lamp, a bird), none from the flash. In the shop, `stormTick` keeps the storm going and a flash lightens the room by fog_light at 0.1.
292. **Thunder (55.3):** `G.audio.engine` cue 'thunder' (a = distance 0..1), fired 1–4 s after its flash (1 + 3 × dist): looped noise through a low-pass at 170 − 80 × dist Hz whose cutoff wobbles (an LFO of 1.3–2.3 Hz, ±50 − 20 × dist Hz), swelling over 0.25 + 0.5 × dist s to 0.06 × (1 − 0.6 × dist) and rolling away over 3–5 s, with a soft band-passed crack (0.01) when nearer than 0.35; on the ambience bus (muffled indoors, under water quieter), under the master limiter, no louder than a big splash. No jumpscare: no sudden loud sound, nothing blocks input, the dog is untouched.
293. **Checks (Phase 55):** `dbg.storm(k)`, `dbg.storm(null)`, `dbg.storm()`, `dbg.flash()`, an overlay line (storm k, flashes, next). Headless Chromium (`?debug&seed=7`, Fogbound at 22:00, storm held at 1): flashes come, thunder is heard after them (`heard` counts), the storm hours per water as listed, a flash shows in the shop (0.19 just after), 60.3 fps over 120 frames with three flashes, no page errors; screenshots looked at (no moon, thick clouds, a bolt behind the hills). Presentation only: the replay hash and every Node check are unchanged.

294. **The ask (Phase 56, 2026-10-31):** "Improve tackle shop and cabin. The cabin should be the same idea as the tackle shop, cozy with a lot of details, the pixel density, resolution and detail of the main scenes at the lakes, a lot of colour palettes but keeping that old, lofi, Mac DeMarco-like cabin with music instruments, liminal, PlayStation 1. The tackle shop should have more detail keeping the vibe and rusty; the main character and the dog more real shape and details." Not in §4; built as one phase, presentation only (`js/sim` and `data/` untouched, the replay hash and every Node check unchanged).
295. **Interior palette (56.1):** 52 keys added to `sprites.js`: 70s panelling (`panel_*`), mustard, burnt orange, olive, dusty pink, lavender (4 each), a CRT's blue (`crt_*`), chrome (4), brick (3), plant greens (`leaf_deep` … `leaf_lit`; the old `leaf_dim/rust/gold` are autumn trees and kept), a guitar's sunburst (`sun_*`), vinyl, a dim pink neon, ivory, `denim_lit` and a warm near-black `outline`. None has a sky or water prefix, so the dusk ceiling is untouched. They are listed in `INTERIOR`, which `shift()` skips, so fish portraits and every earlier sprite keep their exact colours.
296. **`G.gfx.pix` (56.1):** a grid of palette keys to paint small things on before they reach a canvas: shaded ellipses (`ell`), capsules (`cap`), polygons with a shading function (`poly`), slabs, lines, patterns over what is painted (`over`), an outline that follows the silhouette (`outline`), and `paint` in runs of one key a row. Bake time only. It is how the people and the props get real shapes with readable edges under 8.3 #84.
297. **The people (56.2, `js/ui/room.js`):** the fisher 32×48 (was 32×32), feet at y 46: a figure in proportion (head ~1/4, shoulders, a tapering jacket with collar, lapels, zip, chest pockets and folds, sleeves with cuffs and hands, creased trousers with knees, boots with laces and soles), each hat facing down, up and to the side, the face with ears, brows, eyes with their light, nose and mouth, a beard if the look has one; strides lift a boot and swing the opposite hand; side view scissors the legs. The dog 32×32 from the front and behind, 40×32 from the side: a skull, the stop, a muzzle, nose and mouth, a deep chest, a tucked belly, hind legs with thigh and angled hock, forelegs, paws, the breed's ears, tail and coat (the golden's feathering, the poodle's curls, the husky's mask, the St. Bernard's blaze), a sitting pose with the haunch folded. Rusty 32×48: an olive trucker cap with a cream front and a peeling fish patch, grey tufts, round glasses over bagged eyes, drooping brows, a red nose, a yellowing beard and moustache, a red flannel with its check, overalls with brass buckles, a bib pocket and pencil, a patch and a stain, a belly and a ring; reading, sipping, blinking. Shadows under everyone. The walk itself (`walker(map, objects, mat)`) is lifted from Phase 36 unchanged in its rules and serves both rooms; `G.ui.shopRoom.sprite/prebake` now call `G.ui.room`.
298. **The kit (56.3, `js/ui/room_kit.js`):** planked floors (boards in staggered lengths, each its own tone, grain strokes, knots, nail heads, light read per board), a 70s panelled wall (beam, tall boards with grooves and water stains, chair rail, wainscot, baseboard) and a log wall (round logs lit along the top, chinking, gaps while derelict), wall tops and the doorway to the night, windows (a frame and sill baked; the glass live: the lake's sky in eight bands at the hour, far hills and pines, the water with the sky in it, fog, rain on the glass, lightning, stars at night, a figure for 39.3), string lights (a sagging cable baked, bulbs live that breathe, with a small glow at night), a night mask with stepped pools, a stepped round lamp glow, rugs (concentric 70s bands, a braided oval, a runner), and props painted on grids: an acoustic guitar (sunburst or natural), an electric, an amp, a synth on an X stand, a record crate with its player (the record turns live), a CRT with walnut sides and rabbit ears (a fishing show or static live), a boombox, potted plants (monstera, fern, snake plant, trailing pothos), a floor lamp, a banker's lamp, mugs, cans, books.
299. **The shop (56.4):** the same 12×8 map, objects and walk as Phase 36–43 (every shelf, Rusty and the door where they were), with the back wall now 44 px taller above row 0 (the room is drawn at (128, 8), 384×300). Repainted in the kit: the panelled wall with its beam of bulbs, two windows (a dim BAIT neon that flickers every 17 s in one, plants on both sills), the perch trophy, a torn fish chart, the clock and a calendar, a pegboard of faded lure packets with an empty hook; the rack's rail and slotted base (the rods live, longer, with guides and reels), the jar shelf (peeled labels, the unlabelled jar's teal glint), the lure case, a fern on a stool with a landing net, the gear shelf; behind the counter a coffee maker, a boombox and tapes, a CRT showing a fishing show, a stack of cans; the bait station of cooler, cricket cage (mesh live) and aerated tank. Four new blocking tiles where nothing could be faced before: a fern (5,1), the guitar on its amp (7,1, Space strums a chord), a chest freezer (1,6), a monstera (10,6); and a floor lamp (3,4) that replaces the hanging lamp (a pendant read as an object on the floor in this view). The counter is a prop drawn in order of feet with the people, so Rusty stands behind it from the waist down; its leg has a glass display of lures; on it the till, a bell, an ashtray (smoke live), a paper and a jar of sweets. Steam, bubbles, the clock's hands and 39.3's strange are as before.
300. **The cabin (56.5):** a room you walk into, like the shop: 12×8 tiles, the door at (4,7), log walls. Space does what the thing you face does: the workbench opens the ledger (Space pays for the next stage through `economy.restoreCabin`, as the old Space did), the mirror opens the fisher-and-dog picker (the old A key; A now walks), the trophy wall (two rows of five plaques over columns 5–9) reads the two fish above you, the guitar and amp strum a chord, the synth plays one, the record player plays its side (turning for 9 s), the CRT switches on, the stove, the chair and the bed say a line. The stages: derelict (holes in the floor to the night, gaps in the chinking, a cracked window boarded across, cobwebs, a torn calendar, crates for a bench, a sleeping bag on a pallet, a bucket under a drip, an upturned bucket for a chair, a cold rusted stove, a natural guitar on a crate, a cracked mirror), patched (new boards over the holes, a cot, a workbench with a pegboard of tools, a vise, the ledger and a lantern, a band poster, a shelf of books and tapes with the clock stopped at 3:33), the stove lit (fire in the firebox live, a kettle, split logs in a rack, an orange corduroy armchair, the amp, a sunburst guitar, a monstera, a pothos on the sill), rug and lamp (a braided oval rug, the floor lamp, the CRT with a grey console and tapes, the synth, the record player and speaker, string lights), restored (pink curtains, a patchwork quilt and a wooden headboard, a teal chair with a quilt over it, a painting of the lake with a lit window, a lavender runner). Each stage keeps the same blocked tiles, so the walk never changes. Light: cold moonlight at derelict, the lantern, then the stove's pool, then the lamp's; the night mask per stage. The trophies are decided in `enter()` as before (heaviest first, the stage's cap, the rest in the icebox, unease turns some to face the door); the stage, the coins and the next repair stay in a panel under the room. `dbg`: `G.ui.cabin.state()` (now with where he stands and what he faces) and `G.ui.cabin.walkPath('UDLR')`.
301. **Sound and checks (Phase 56):** three cues in `G.audio.engine`: 'strum' (a chord of Cmaj7, Am7, Fmaj7 or G6 plucked low to high, 35 ms apart, a little out of tune), 'keys' (the chord together, a slow attack), 'record' (the needle's crackle, then the four chords once over eight seconds); all quiet (gains 0.003–0.011, under the dog's bark), through the limiter. Both rooms prebake the fisher and the dog on entry so no step bakes a frame. Checks: every Node check as in 8.5 (`--replay 12345` 1966984761, pacing 6.0 / 45.1 / 345.6). Headless Chromium (`?debug&seed=7`): in the shop `dbg.shopWalk` reaches Rusty from below (9,4), the rack (2,2), the guitar (7,2) and the tank (2,5); in the cabin from the mat the bench at (1,2) opens the ledger, Space pays 150c and Derelict becomes Patched Roof, the new plaques fill only on the next visit (2 → 3 mounted), the plaques read from (5,1), the mirror opens `look` from 'cabin', the TV switches on by Space, the arrow keys walk and Down off the mat goes back to `sailing`; the `shop` and `looks top` gallery pages show the room and every look's sprites; no page errors. Draw cost median 0.1 ms in both rooms (bake on entry 35–90 ms, inside the travel fade); headless Chromium stalls ~50 ms on some frames in every scene (the lake every 11 frames, the rooms every 225), a property of its software canvas, not of the rooms.

302. **The ask and the plan (Phase 57, 2026-11-01):** the user asked for the game to be playable from a PC as now or from a phone, planned before anything else is added. [PLAN_MOBILE.md](PLAN_MOBILE.md) has four phases: 57 the page on a phone, 58 thumb controls (bigger buttons, a pad and an action button that says what it does, the rooms' pad), 59 every key by touch (a tap on the row you want in every overlay, the still picture, full screen in the pause list), 60 fitting it in (a phone's frame under CPU throttle, a gallery page, HANDOFF notes). Its rule: the PC game, its fit and `js/sim`/`data` do not change.
303. **Touch mode and the fit (57.1):** `G.core.device` (new, `js/core/device.js`, loaded after `input.js`) owns the canvas's size and place; `index.html`'s `fit()` is gone and the canvas is placed by `left`/`top` instead of a CSS transform. Touch mode is a coarse pointer (`matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)')`) or a touch seen (a touch laptop's first touch). Not in touch mode the fit is exactly the old one: the largest whole-number scale in device pixels, fractional below 1×, centred (checked against the old formula at 1920×1080, 1366×657, 1280×720 @2 and 600×400). In touch mode the area is the screen less the safe-area insets (`viewport-fit=cover`; the insets read from a fixed probe with `env(safe-area-inset-*)` padding), and the whole-number scale is kept only while it fills ≥ 95% of the limiting side (`FILL`), else the fractional scale, nearest-neighbour: at 3× a game pixel is 3 or 4 device pixels, which does not show, while 85% (the first try) left 844×390 at 92% and 915×412 at 87%. Refits on resize, orientation change, the visual viewport's resize and full-screen changes.
304. **The page (57.2):** `user-scalable=no`, `touch-action: none` and `overscroll-behavior: none` on the page (the canvas had it already, the black margins did not), no selection, callout or tap highlight. The first `touchend` in touch mode (a user activation on iOS, where `touchstart` is not) asks for full screen (`navigationUI: 'hide'`) and then `screen.orientation.lock('landscape')`, each in try/catch; once per load, so a player who leaves full screen is not asked again, and never from the home screen (`standalone`). `manifest.webmanifest` (`display: fullscreen`, `orientation: landscape`, black, no icons: Phase 60.2) and the iOS/Android web-app metas. iPhones have no element full screen; there the home screen is the way (HANDOFF, Phase 60.3).
305. **Portrait (57.3):** the game is landscape only (PLAN_MOBILE.md §3). In touch mode with the screen taller than wide, `device.drawNotice` draws "Turn your phone sideways / The lake is wide." in a title-less `G.ui.panel` frame at 3× in the middle of the picture (at 0.61 CSS px a game px, 1× text is unreadable), after the buttons. Nothing is dimmed and input goes through: a tap still casts.
306. **Touch fixes (57.4):** "Fishing in another tab" (12.5) was cleared only by `mousedown` or a key, and `input.js` cancels a touch's mouse events, so a phone could not take its save back: `touchend` on the canvas reclaims too, and the line says "Tap to fish here." in touch mode. The audio unlocks on `touchend`, `pointerup` and `click` as well as before, and a context in any state but running or closed (iOS's `interrupted` after a call or the lock screen) resumes on the next gesture while the page is shown. `G.audio.engine.state()`.
307. **dbg and checks (57.5):** `dbg.device()` (touch, coarse, portrait, fullscreen, standalone, viewport, dpr, insets, scale, whole, css size and place, fill, fsAsked and its result, the lock, fits, the audio's state) and an overlay line `device touch|pc [portrait] WxH @dpr scale … fill …% [fullscreen …]`. `js/sim` and `data/` are untouched, so every Node check is as in 8.5.

308. **The touch layout (58.1, `input.js`):** in touch mode (`G.core.device.touch()`) `hit()` reads a second layout, `input.layout()`, filled into preallocated records each call (`TB` buttons, `PAD`, `ACT`, `LAY`); with a mouse only, the old `BUTTONS` at the bottom right and the `mouseNear` rule are unchanged. Sizes come from the CSS px a game px takes now (`device.scale()`): keys 44 CSS px (`BTN_CSS`), the boat's pad 52, the rooms' pad 44, the action button 64, clamped in game px (28–64, 28–72, 40–88); at 844×390 that is 41, 48 and 60 game px. What shows depends on `where()`: 'boat' (sailing, no overlay, card or setup panel): J M S P in a row at the top right (8 px from the edges, 6 apart; ESC is left out there since P does what it did on the boat), the pad and the action button; 'room' (the shop or the cabin, no text box, window or panel open: their new `busy()`): ESC at the corner, the four-way pad and the action button; 'menu' (anything open): ESC alone at the corner, and taps go to the zones and the panels (card keys, setup rows) as before; '' (the gallery, `?compact`): nothing. A deviation from the plan's 58.1: the row is not on screen with every overlay, because over the setup panel (up to 320 px, top centre) and the catch card it would cover them, and none of J M S P is any use there. The depth readout moves under the row in touch mode. The old zones still work behind the new keys (a tap in the middle casts); the pad comes first in `zone()`, so its right arrow (inside the old left zone) steers right.
309. **Drawing (58.2, `hud.drawTouch`):** keys and pad keys as the old buttons (ink at 0.7, a 1 px `fog_dark` rim, amber while held), arrows as rows of pixels, the round button baked for its size (up: `fog_mid` rim on ink; held: `signal_amber` on `rust_dark`), rebaked only when the size changes. On the boat they fade with the HUD's idle fade (P1-7) to 0.35, never out; the round button is at 0.4 with no word, 0.85 with one.
310. **The word (58.3):** `sailing.actionWord()` from the state only: the card's choice (SELL, KEEP, RELEASE), PICK with the setup panel, CAST stopped or charging (nothing while the boat moves, when a press does nothing), REEL sinking, waiting or with interest, HOOK with a fish on the bite, REEL hooked or fighting, nothing casting or landing. `shop.word()`: TALK at Rusty, LOOK at a shelf, PLAY at the guitar. `cabin.word()`: FIX (the bench), LOOK (the mirror, and wherever the hint says look), READ (the plaques), PLAY, TV, WARM, SIT, REST. Drawn in the bold fine font inside the button, not under it as the plan said (there is no room under a button 8 px from the bottom). `G.ui.hud.word()`, and `dbg.device()` gains `layout` and `word`.
311. **The rooms (58.4):** the pad becomes a cross of four (up, left, right, down) at the bottom left; at 844×390 it ends at x 131, 3 px over the shop's outer wall (the room starts at x 128), never over its floor. Held, it walks through the room's own held-key rules, as the arrow keys do. The action button talks and uses; ESC leaves. While a text box or window is open the cross and the button hide (`busy()`), and the box's taps go to the menu zones as in Phase 40 until 59.2.
312. **What did not move:** `js/sim` and `data/` are untouched (every Node check as in 8.5); a PC with a mouse sees no touch layout (`showButtons()` false until the mouse comes near, `hit(600, 340)` still the old ESC, nothing at the pad's place).

313. **The key table (59.1):** PLAN_MOBILE.md §2b lists every key each scene and overlay reads (from the code) with its touch. Two gaps it found besides the plan's: the shop's and the cabin's text boxes sit along the bottom, where the menu zones gave `down`, so a tap on the box itself did nothing (only the middle of the screen advanced it); and the cabin's ledger pays on the action, so a tap anywhere on it must not be the action.
314. **The row rule (59.2):** in touch mode `input.zone()` asks the open overlay (`G.ui.menu.tap`, which hands it to the overlay's `tap(gx, gy)`), or the shop's or cabin's box (`shop.tap`, `cabin.tap`, while `busy()`), after the buttons, the card and the setup panel and before the dog and the zones. Each `tap` may move its own cursor (as `setup.tap` and `card.tap` do since Phases 45–46) and returns what to press: `action`, a direction, `back`, or the new `none` (an action nothing reads: the tap only moved the cursor, or missed). The rule: a tap on a row selects it, a tap on the selected row chooses it, so a stray tap never travels, buys or opens a prompt. A tap off an open overlay is `none` (before, off-panel taps fell to the zones and the middle chose the selected row). The mouse path is unchanged: a click on a PC still does what it did (checked: a click on the pause list chooses the keys' row, Resume).
315. **Each overlay (59.2):** pause: the rows; on the selected Volume row a tap on the ticks left of the level is `left`, at or right of it `right`. Map: a stop's mark (±10 px) or its name by the row rule; the card's SPACE hint goes, its M hint closes. Picker: a cell's left or right third turns that look (and makes it the row), its middle only picks the row; the ESC hint is done. Journal: the location or rarity key turns its filter (`right` with that focus), a tile selects and reads its page, the scrollbar's upper or lower half pages the grid a screen (25 entries) up or down, the J hint closes. Shop: a tap on Rusty's box is Space (the page, the next page, then the choice); Buy/Talk/Leave and the item window's rows by the row rule; the item panel's SPACE hint chooses, its ESC hint goes back. Cabin: a tap on a message box is Space; on the ledger only its SPACE hint pays and its ESC hint is not now. The plan's "the journal turns its pages by a tap on its left or right half" did not fit the journal as built (Phase 54 made it a grid with two filters, not pages), so the grid's own parts take the taps.
316. **The still picture by touch (59.3):** a touch on P in touch mode is held back until it lifts: shorter than 0.6 s (`LONG`) it is P (pressed and let go in one step), longer it presses `still` (H) while the finger is still down, by a timer in `input.js`. With the still picture up nothing is drawn, but P's corner still takes the long press that brings the HUD back. Other touches work as usual while it is up (on the keyboard H blocks nothing either, §0.14).
317. **Full screen in the pause list (59.3):** a 'Full screen on/off' row after Dog barks, only where `device.canFullscreen()` (touch mode, an element full screen, not from the home screen: so not on an iPhone's Safari); the panel grows a row (152 px) and the message moves down with it, built in `open()`. Choosing it flips `save.settings.fullscreen` (new default `true`, filled in on load as Phase 42's `dogBark` was: no version bump) and asks for or leaves full screen from the step after the tap (Chrome's transient activation still holds). With it off, the first tap of a visit no longer asks (57.2).
318. **What did not move:** `js/sim` and `data/` are untouched; the save's version is 6 as before; every Node check as in 8.5. Phase 57's and 58's browser scripts re-run on the final code: 22 and 17 PASS.

319. **A phone's frame (60.1):** measured in headless Chromium on an 844×390 phone (3×) with the CPU throttled (CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`), the debug text hidden: at 1× every frame holds 60 fps (lake at night with an anomaly: draw 6.4 ms, the reflection 4.6 of it; under water 1.0 ms; the shop 0.5 ms); at 4× the lake falls to 12–13 fps (draw 30–34 ms, the reflection 20–28), under water to 22–24 fps, the shop stays at 60; at 6× the lake is at 7–8 fps. A CPU profile at 4× shows 58% (lake) and 77% (under water) of the time in "(program)": the canvas rasterised in software, because headless Chromium has no GPU. A phone's browser draws a canvas this size on its GPU, so these numbers overstate a real phone by an unknown amount; the one cost that is ours regardless is the reflection, which draws everything above the waterline a second time each frame and copies it back in 140+ rows.
320. **The phone budget (60.1):** `G.core.device` checks the frame rate once a second; in touch mode, two seconds running under 50 fps (`SLOW_FPS`) raise the level by one, to 2 (`MAX_LEVEL`), never back within a visit (so it cannot flicker between levels), and never on a PC (checked: a PC throttled 6× at 10 fps stays at 0). `G.gfx.water.setBudget(level)`: level 1 draws the scene into the mirror buffer every other frame and shows the last buffer between (the rows still shift every frame, so the water moves as before); level 2 every third frame, and the rows are copied two view px at a time. A render cache only: nothing the simulation reads. Measured: draw at 1× 6.1 → 3.3 → 2.3 ms, the reflection 7.0 → 4.3 → about 0 between rebuilds; at 4× draw 33.7 → 17.9 → 13.1 ms and 12 → 17 fps (the rest is the software raster, #319). Level 0 and level 2 cannot be told apart in a still (screenshots of both). The plan's "fewer far shoals" was left out: under water our own drawing is 1–5 ms and the rest is the raster. `dbg.budget(n)` holds a level, `dbg.budget(null)` gives it back, `dbg.budget()` reads it and when it rose; `dbg.device()` and the overlay line show it.
321. **The gallery (60.2):** two pages at the end: `touch` (the J M S P ESC keys, the four pad arrows, the round button with each of its 17 words and with none, one of each held, at the sizes for the screen it is on, with those sizes written under them) and `notice` (the home-screen icon at 3×, and the portrait notice as an upright phone sees it, `device.drawNotice(ctx, true)`). `hud.touchParts` hands the gallery the HUD's own painters.
322. **The icon (60.2):** `device.paintIcon(px)` paints a 32×32 grid of palette keys scaled up whole (a dusk sky to `dusk_amber`, a `dusk_high` moon so nothing passes the dusk ceiling, pines, the water with the moon's path, the boat with the fisher, the dog and the rod); at boot it is set as the page's `icon` and `apple-touch-icon` as a PNG data URL (2.7 KB). The manifest has none: Chrome installs only from an icon at a same-origin URL and the project has no image files (§0.2): `TODO(P60.2)` in `device.js`. Whether iOS takes the data URL for the home screen is not checked on a device.
323. **How to play on a phone (60.3)** is step 7 of HANDOFF.md's "How to continue": `--bind 0.0.0.0`, the computer's address, holding it sideways, adding it to the home screen, the controls, saves (over plain `http://` the clipboard is refused, so Export save shows the string to copy). The PC played through by a script at 1280×720 with no touch, once by keyboard and once by mouse: a cast, a gloam perch hooked, fought and landed, sold (0 → 6 coins), J, M, P and S opening their panels and Esc closing them; no touch layout, the pause list at its old 7 rows, the budget at 0.

324. **The ask (Phase 61, 2026-11-01):** "solve the known issues please", after the four open items of 8.4 the phone plan left (#34–#37). #34 (play it on a real phone) needs a phone and is left to the user; the other three are done, with one more found while checking them (#38). Not a phase of a plan file; one commit as a phase.
325. **The hints on a phone (#36):** `G.ui.panel.hint`/`hintW` show a label through `shown()`: in touch mode SPACE is TAP, W/S and A/D are left out (rows are tapped), 'W/S A/D' (the journal) is TAP, and J, M and S as "close" are ESC; on a PC every label is as before (checked: pause W/S A/D, journal W/S A/D J, map M, picker W/S A/D ESC, setup SPACE A/D S). Phase 59's taps measure hints with `hintW`, so they follow (the ledger pays on the narrower TAP cap; every Phase 57–60 script re-run). `panel.shown` is exported for checks. The pause list on a phone shows no hint (both were arrow keys).
326. **The icon files (#37):** `node tools/icon.js` paints `G.core.device.ICON` (the 32×32 grid of palette keys, now exported; the gallery's notice page still paints it in the browser) with the palette of `sprites.js`, loaded as the game loads them, scaled up whole, and writes `icon-192.png` (718 bytes) and `icon-512.png` (2,505 bytes) with Node's zlib; `--check` fails if the files are missing or differ from the grid. They are listed in `manifest.webmanifest` and linked as the page's `icon` and `apple-touch-icon` (the Phase 60 data URL set at boot is gone, and its `TODO(P60.2)`). Chromium's `Page.getInstallabilityErrors` on the phone page: none (it named the missing icon before). These are the project's first image files, against §0.2's "no external assets": they are generated from the palette by a committed tool rather than drawn, which the user's ask for #37 accepted (8.4 #37 named it as the way).
327. **`--rod`'s cost (#35):** the check's 0.05 ms limit was not the machine: `rod.js` ran in an empty `vm` context, where every global (`Math`, `G`) is looked up through Node's sandbox, and a solve cost 0.052 ms there against 0.036 with the host's `Math` and typed arrays in the context (the browser has no such lookup). `rodCheck` now gives its context `Math`, `Float32Array` and `Float64Array`, and times the best of 7 batches of 2,000 solves rather than one of 5,000 (a batch can also time the garbage of the checks before it, or another process). The limit is unchanged. On this machine: 0.035–0.039 ms in three runs, 15 PASS; every other rod number as before.
328. **The ESC key over open panels (#38, found while checking #36):** in Phase 58 ESC alone went to the top-right corner whatever was open, and covered the shop's item window's coins (and the top-right corner of the map's and the journal's panels). `input.layout()` now puts it in the first corner (top right, bottom right, top left, bottom left) that covers none of the open panels (each gives preallocated `rects()`: the map, the journal, the shop, the cabin; the pause list, the picker, the card and the setup panel their `rect()`; the HUD's corner on the water) and hides it where every corner is covered (the map and the journal: their ESC hints close them by a tap, Phase 59). Checked on two phones over eleven states: ESC over 0 panels in each (the shop's items at the bottom right, the rest at the top right), the map and the journal closed by their hints. The depth readout still drops under ESC in the top-right corner.

329. **The ask (Phase 62, 2026-11-01):** "find issues that are not solved and solve them please". The repository had no open GitHub issues, so the work is the open items of 8.4 that code can settle: #28, #24 and #25. The rest are watches (#6, #18, #20, #26, #31), by design or accepted (#7, #9, #30), the user's (#29, #34), or optional art and tooling (#10, #21, #32), and were left as they were.
330. **Head shakes and the rod's load (#28):** `rod.kick(amount)` now scales the impulse by the load the blank holds, `min(1, st.load / K.kickLoad)` with `kickLoad` 0.5: a shake reaches the rod through the line, so on a slack or lightly loaded rod it is a knock, not a swing through straight (the kick's direction, the sign of the settled bend, means nothing near straight anyway). Measured with sailing.js's largest kick (5 rad/s at the tip) every 0.12 s for 3 s, the lines 50° down and the hands at 58°: at load 0.05 the tip swung 34° from its settled bend before and 5.0° now on the cane rod and the Long Rod; at 0.8 and up the swing is unchanged (49.8°). `--rod` has a 16th check: under 6° and never through straight at load 0.05, still 20°+ at load 0.8 (it FAILs on the old `rod.js`: 49.8° light).
331. **The journal's first opening (#24):** since Phase 54 the book is gone and the opening cost 37–51 ms in headless Chromium (the grid's portraits; 8.4 said ~90 ms for the book). `journal.warm(n)` bakes n of the grid's portraits (every species, silhouette and colour: 92), and index.html's draw asks for one a frame until it returns true, into `fishgen`'s own cache, off screen (no game state, no `G.vrng`). Each call measured 0.8–1.1 ms median, at most 4.9–5.9 ms, for the first ~1.3 s after boot; the first opening then cost 5.7–13.9 ms. The entry's plate picture (one at a time, up to 15 ms each to bake) is still baked when it is first shown, as before; warming all 92 of them would have cost a frame or two each at boot.
332. **The old sprites (#25):** the `owl`, `moon` and `hud_coin` sprite definitions (Phase 10, replaced by painted art in Phase 18) are removed from `sailing.js` and `hud.js`; nothing drew them but the gallery's sprite list.

333. **The ask (Phases 63–67, 2026-11-01):** the dog in the shop smaller, in real proportion; the cabin and the shop alive (a lava lamp, a PlayStation, more details, reflections); lures that move as real ones on the retrieve, more lures (a red-and-white bobber with bait under it), a more real cast; a more detailed map with better navigation; the pier at one side of the lake, from which the cabin and the shop are reached. Planned in PLAN_PLACES.md, one phase for each.
334. **The dog to scale (Phase 63):** the rooms' dog was 40×32 beside a 32×48 fisher, two thirds of his height. Shrinking the baked sprite would lose single pixels (eyes, nose) and blur the outline, so the same painter runs through `G.gfx.pix.scaled(g, s)`, a view of a smaller grid that maps every shape's coordinates and radii by s (radii floored at about half a pixel so legs stay whole) and hands pattern callbacks the painter's own coordinates. `size` in `data/looks.js` (golden 0.70, husky 0.68, St. Bernard 0.80, poodle 0.66; validated 0.55–0.85): the golden's back now stands 15 px against his 46. `room.anchor(kind, dir, lk)` and the shadow follow it; the boat's side-view dog (js/scene/dog.js) was already in proportion and is unchanged.

335. **What makes a room lived in (Phase 64):** the user named a lava lamp, a PlayStation, more details and reflections. New parts in `js/ui/room_kit.js`: `lavaLamp` (drawn live: three wax blobs on 19, 26 and 33 s loops in a tapered glass, a pink glow at night; off, the wax sits in a cold pool), `console90s` and `pad90s` (a grey console of the late 90s: disc lid, open and reset, a power light, a memory card; named generically, no brand), `console90sScreen` (1 s of the tube warming, a four-colour diamond logo growing, then a fishing game in polygons that jitter at 8 Hz), `boxFan`/`fanBlades`, `fridge`, `cooler`/`coolerInside` (a flicker once in 46 s), `cat` (three baked frames: breathing, a tail twitch every 11 s, an ear every 17 s). Props gain an optional `live(ctx, x, y, t, env)` drawn right after their image in the feet order, so a figure in front of a table still covers its lamp.
336. **The cabin's new tiles:** the fan (5,1), the fridge (9,1), the side table (6,4) and the console (7,3) block at every stage (a crate, a dead fridge, a crate or a box of cables stand there before the stage that brings them), so the walk is the same at every stage as 8.3 #296 asked. Space: the fan and the lava lamp switch, the fridge has two lines, the console is the TV's. The shop's freezer (the cat) and the new cooler (10,5) get a line each in Rusty's box (`from = 'thing'` closes it instead of going on to Buy, Talk, Leave).
337. **Reflections:** `K.reflect` lays a stretched, stepped streak (2 px rows, fading as the square of the distance, narrowing to 45%) on the boards under a lit thing, before the standing things are drawn, so they cover it; the lamps' pools in the night mask are clear, so it reads at night. Under the cabin's bench lantern, floor lamp, stove, lava lamp and the TV when on; under the shop's floor lamp and cooler. `K.glassLamps` puts two warm smudges low in each window at night, `K.glare` a curved glint on each CRT.

338. **The retrieve (Phase 65.1):** reeling used to move the lure across at 30 px/s and up at 22 px/s whatever its depth. Now `c.rv` (the reel's speed) spins up at 7/s and runs down at 4/s, and the lure moves at it along the line toward the bent rod tip (`boat.rodTip`), so deep and close it climbs steeply and far out it runs nearly level; at the surface far out it skitters, and it lifts out only within 14 px of home. On top: the spinner rises 0.3 px/s per px/s (the blade), the plug dives to a running depth of its maxDepthM × speed/30 and floats up when paused, the jig hops 2.2 times a second and drops, the grub runs at 55% of the line's rise, the spoon at 70%. Paused (`RV.still` 0.6 px/s), a sinking lure falls back: for 1.6 s on a tight line it swings in a pendulum round the tip (the line's length held), then straight down to its own depth; a live bait swings and then hangs, as before. `dbg.lureBody()` adds retrieve, swing and depthM.
339. **The bobber and the spoon (65.2):** `swim: 'float'` with `carries: 'worm'`: the float lies on the surface and the worm sinks to maxDepthM under it; the line ends at the float (`lurePos`), a dotted leader runs down to the worm, the float bobs on a nibble and is pulled under on a bite (`underwater.drawFloat`). Fish take a rig as the bait it carries (`fish_ai.interest` via `carries`); the validator checks a rig carries an unlimited bait and that every water has a cane-rod fish that takes each lure. Tuned with `--tackle`: at 2.5 m the bobber earned 33% of worms in the Pines, at 4.5 m 43%; at 6 m (a slip bobber) and attract 1.2 it earns 57–151%. The spoon (`'flutter'`, sink 1.4) is in every species' lures that has the spinner, at the spinner's weight. Both sold at the lure case with their own icons.
340. **The cast (65.3):** the flight's horizontal path is eased by 1 − e^(−1.7u) (normalised to land on x1, the drag scaled down for short casts) while the height stays a parabola in time, so the lure leaves fast, tops out early and drops steeply; flights are a little longer (0.5 s + d/240) and higher (12 + 0.22 d px). The splash is × a weight per hook (`SPLASH`: the float 1, a cricket 0.35). Landing x is unchanged (`landingX`).
341. **The replay:** the Node session only reads a lure's depth (min of the water's and maxDepthM) and fish.lures, so the hash stays `1966984761`; `--tackle` gains the bobber and spoon rows.

342. **The chart's detail (Phase 66.1):** baked once in `paintChart` with the rest: hachures under every peak, a dotted contour 9 px outside each lake and a depth contour at 42% of its radii, the deepest sounding in metres (maxDepth × 0.25) beside each lake's mark, rivers (`RIVERS`: creek to Fogbound, creek to the Pines, the Red Room to the creek, the Pines' outflow) as winding 2 px lines, the shore road (`ROAD`) dashed with a bridge where the outflow crosses it, the pier and the dotted paths to the shop and the cabin (moved to Fogbound's west shore for Phase 67), a legend (by water, river, road, deepest) and a 2 km scale bar (`KM` 0.022 km a chart px), and a border ticked every 16 px. Trees keep off the legend and the shore houses; the dotted route now joins only waters.
343. **Navigation (66.2):** the arrows used to step through the list in order (up and left back, down and right on). Now `toward(from, ax, ay)` picks the stop ahead along the arrow within about 60° (across ≤ 1.75 × along), the nearest by along + 2 × across; none that way, nothing moves. The amber brackets ease to the chosen stop (14/s). The card adds the distance from the water you are on ("2.9 km from here") and each water's deepest. `dbg.mapNav()` walks the four arrows from every stop: every stop reaches every other, and no press lands on a stop behind it.
